5.3.2 The Committee sought to know about the The Zirakpur - Tribune Chowk Road Project. The 
Administration of UT of Chandigarh replied that the matter is pending due to a PIL filed in Hon‟ble 
High Court of Punjab and Haryana. However, the project is being funded by the Ministry of  Road 
Transport & Highway as this stretch of road is on National Highway-5.  The tender for the work 
was invited, and LoA was issued to the lowest bidder on 19 th November, 2019.  Meanwhile, the 
Run Club filed a PIL in Hon‟ble Punjab & Haryana High Court regarding cutting down of trees, 
which are coming in the alignment of the project.  The Hon‟ble High Court has granted stay on the 
project.  As such, the matter is pending in the Hon‟ble Punjab & Haryana High Court and the next 
date of hearing is 26th April, 2021. 
 
5.3.3 The Committee also enquired about the progress made in the approach Road to Chandigarh 
Airport. The Administration of UT of Chandigarh informed the Committee that earlier, road 
distance from Tribune Chowk to the Chandigarh Airport was 2.71 km
.  Now, existing route from 
Tribune Chowk to Chandigarh International Airport is approx. 14.52 km through Punjab state 
(Mohali), which is causing difficulty to the residents of Chandigarh as they have to cover more 
distance and spend more  time to reach the Airport.  To cut short this road distance, certain options 
were proposed / deliberated, from time to time.  One of the options was to connect the old and ne w 
terminal through an underpass below the existing runway. This was not agreed to by the Air Force 
Authorities. In Chandigarh Master Plan (CMP) 2031, another proposal of road connectivity to 
Airport was given from Sector 48 (Purv Marg) through village Jagatpura, which will be 
approximately 4.7 kms from Tribune chowk, thereby, reducing the distance by about 10km from 
Tribune Chowk for Chandigarh residents. For this proposal, land acquisition may be required in 
Punjab, so this needs to be done by Punjab Government. 
5.3.4 The Committee notes that existing route from Tribune Chowk to Chandigarh 
International Airport is causing unnecessary inconvenience to the residents of Chandigarh as 
they have to cover more distance and spend more time to reach the airport. The  Committee 
recommends that the MHA may take up this matter with Ministries of Civil Aviation and 
Defence on priority basis to connect the old and new terminal through an underp ass below 
the existing runway to make it convenient for the people travelling through the Chandigarh 

74 
 
Airport. Alternatively, the Committee further recommends that the MHA may coordinate 
with Government of Punjab for making provision of  approximately 4.7 kms road through  
village Jagatpura for Chandigarh Airport which will reduce the distance to airport by about 
10 km and will save time of large number of people travelling through airport from 
Chandigarh . 
  
5.4 Demand No. 53 - Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu 
 
5.4.1 The Gross demand projected by the U.T of Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu for 
the year 2021-22 was Rs.4,037.51 crore; out of which Budget of Rs.3,563.59 crores has been 
provided to the U.T. There is a short fall of Rs.473.92 crore as compared to the projection made. 
The allocation is to be utilized mainly for Developmental of Educational, Health and Other 
infrastructure along with focus on Social upliftment and empowerment and for the upliftment and 
support to Local bodies i.e. District Panchayat and Municipal Councils of U.T. of Dadra and Nagar 
Haveli and Daman and Diu and also UT Share for Smart Cities of Silvassa and Diu. 
 
5.4.2 In a reply to a questionnaire, the Administration of UT relplied that the schemes/activities 
that are likely to be affected due to reduction in allocation is mainly for Construction of Medical 
College at Silvassa and Nursing College at Daman, Construction of High Level Bridge at Dudh ani 
– Kauncha across Damanganga river at Silvassa, Fly over with approach and service roads at two 
junction at Silvassa, Sea Front Road and Beautification of Devka Beach at Nani Daman and also at 
Diu, Development of Lake Resort at Gandhipara Diu etc. and some of ongoing works also. 
5.4.3 It is also likely that the matching 50% UT share to Smart cities of Diu and Silvassa, may not 
be given fully, which in turn may affect their ongoing and new works and projects. It will also 
affect release of grants to various societies and Grant in Aid Institutions which in turn will affect 
social sector projects and schemes and activities undertaken by grantee bodies. It might affect 
payment of salary arrears of technical institutions staff and in clearance of Domestic Travel bills.

5.4.5 In the F.Y. 2019-20 the U.T. Administration of Daman and Diu was able to spend 99.97% 
of the allocated RE budget and Dadra and Nagar Haveli was able to spend 99.99% of its allocated 
RE. As per expenditure details upto 31 st December, 2020, expenditure in U.T. of Dadra and Nagar 
Haveli and Daman and Diu has been 59.82% of allocated RE budget. However, the under-
utilization of fund till the month of December, 2020 for the F.Y. 2020-21 is due to pandemic 
situation of COVID-19 during the year which resulted in slow progress of work and 
projects/activities. 
5.4.6 The Committee was informed by the Administration of UT of Dadra & Nagar Haveli and 
Daman & Diu that the main reason for increase in budget allocation is due to the fact that because 
of COVID-19 pandemic, majority of works were held up and that lead to temporary halt in the 
development of projects/ activities which were to be completed in financial year 2020-21. These 
delayed projects/activities are to be completed in the next financial year 2021-22 and therefore, 
there is increase in the allocation during the financial year 2021-22. The details of projects to be

5.4.7 The Committee was informed by the representative of UT of Dadra and Nagar Haveli and 
Daman and Diu that the UT came into existence on 26 th January, 2020 with the merger of erstwhile 
Union Territories of Dadra and Nagar Haveli, and Daman and Diu.  The Union Territory has taken 
steps for creation of physical and social infrastructure, Nand Ghars, health and wellness centres, e-
Aarogya implementation of Ayushman Bharat, Jampore beachfront road, etc. The work for civil 
terminal at Nani-Daman Coast Guard airport has already been awarded.  STP at Moti-Daman has 
already been established.  With regard to catamaran services, SCI has been approac hed and 
feasibility has been examined.  
5.4.8 The Committee was informed that to improve marine connectivity across the islands & 
mainland sector in Union Territory, U.T. Administration has decided to start Catamaran Ferry 
service between Daman & Diu to enable the citizens of the UT as well as tourists to travel from 
Daman & Diu through sea route.  
 
5.4.9 The Committee was informed by the representative of the UT that approval has been given 
for a medical college for 177 students in Silvassa which will enhance medical facilities in UT and 
provide an opportunity to local tribal students to pursue career in medicine.  E-Arogya, a paperless 
cloud-based healthcare service delivery system, has also been launched.  Recently, it has also been 
decided to privatize power infrastructure in the UTs for which bids have been invited. 
5.4.10 The Committee sought to know to know about the Bird Shelter in Jampore in Daman. The 
representative of the UT replied that Bird aviary project is in advanced stage of execution. 
Road connectivity in Badi Daman
 
 
5.4.11 The Committee was informed that the UT Administration of DNH & DD is an Industrial Hub & 
is developing in to a Tourist Destination. Due to such recent developments, the vehicular and tourist 
traffic have increased manifold, leading to vehicular traffic and industrial traffic congestion in the roads 
of Daman District. Therefore, as part of development & future planning, it has been envisaged to 
improve road connectivity in whole Daman District including Moti Daman. The Major road connecting 
Moti Daman is NH-848-B at Bamanpuja, (CH-KM 8/180), which in turn connects to National Highway 
NH-48 at Mohangam Fatak, Vapi (Gujarat). 
 
5.4.12 Total PWD roads in Daman district are 68.50 Km, (50.70 Km MDR & 17.80 Km ODR). Out of 
this, 24.50 Km have already been widened and re-carpeted and the remaining 44.00 Km MDR / ODR 
roads are proposed for strengthening, widening and re-carpeting in the F.Y 2021-2022. Out of this, 
16.00 Km of roads in Moti Daman have been widened and re-carpeted, and 5.00 Km road widening 

82 
 
from Dholar to Jampore up to Varliwad, Moti Daman is under progress. Moreover, all villages, 
settlements are connected with all weather roads in whole Daman District, including Moti Daman area. 
 
Airport expansion in Daman and Diu.
 
 
5.4.13 Presently an air-strip is available at Daman, which is under the control of Coast Guard Air 
Station, Daman. However, there is no Civil Terminal Building to cater to civil flights. Therefore, it was 
envisaged to develop a Civil Terminal Building in Daman as Daman & DNH is an industrial hub, 
wherein, Plastics, Pharmaceutical, Chemical & Chemical Products, Electrical & Electronics industries 
are concentrated. Also, this UT is a well-known tourist destination, where large number of tourists from 
neighboring states such as Gujarat, Maharashtra, and also from other states visit every year.  
 
5.4.14 Further, the Ministry of Civil Aviation, Government of India has awarded 04 routes to the UT 
Administration under Regional Connectivity Scheme UDAN 4.0., viz. (i) Vadodara-Diu-Vadodara (ii) 
Surat-Diu-Surat (iii) Daman-Diu-Daman and (iv) Ahmedabad-Daman-Ahmedabad. Therefore, as an 
interim measure the UT Administration has constructed a Temporary Civil Terminal with all basic 
facilities at the premise of Indian Coast Guard Air Station, Daman. Further, the Temporary Civil 
Terminal has been vetted by Airport Authority of India and Bureau of Civil Aviation Security (BC AS). 
The flight operations are planned to commence on final certification from various agencies/ 
stakeholders involved. 
 
5.4.15 The Committee recommends that the MHA along with the UT Administration may 
construct bridges between Vanakbara and Kotda in Diu, Kalai and Jampore and Kolak and 
Nani Daman, both in Daman. 
5.4.16 The Committee notes that the area of Daman is 72 sq. kms which contains marshy 
land, funnel area, mangroves, rivers and the like.  Other than these areas, the remaining land 
available for development is only 22 sq. kms.  The area available in Diu is still less. Therefore,  
the Committee recommends that that entire Daman be declared under Coastal Regulation 
Zone II (CRZ II) instead of the existing Coastal Regulation Zone III (CRZ III) and Diu as an 
Island. The Committee also recommends that a ring road may be constructed in Daman , for 
which additional funds may be requested at RE stage. 
5.4.17 The Committee recommends that the MHA may take up with the Ministry of 
Railways for stoppage of the Mumbai New Delhi Rajdhani Express (12951/52)  at Vapi Station 
as it caters to the needs of Vapi, Silvassa, Daman and other nearby industrial town.  
5.4.18 The Committee notes that Dadra & Nagar Haveli predominantly is tribal area of the 
UT of  Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu. Therefore, the Committee recommends 
promotion and incentivisation of the arts and crafts of these tribal communities will help in 
generation of revenue and jobs for them. The Committee also recommends to give reservation 
to the residents of the UT for group C & D posts of various departments.  
 
5.5 Demand No. 54 –Ladakh 
 
5.5.1 An amount to the tune of Rs.5,958 crore has been allocated for UT of Ladakh in BE 2021-
22. The Committee sought to know the UT Administration's plans to utilize this allocation in the 
upcoming fiscal year. The Ministry replied that the Union Territory of Ladakh would be spending a 
major share of the Budget 201-22 for infrastructure development of the new UT. The major 

83 
 
projects would be improvement of connectivity and  development of power infrastructure, Besides 
this, agriculture, horticulture and tourism sector would be the focus areas so that the livelihood of 
the people of Ladakh can be substantially enhanced.  
5.5.2 The Committee was informed by the UT administration of Ladakh that after creation of UT 
Ladakh, there has been a great push for the development. Ladakh University and one new degree  
college have started while work on another degree college and upgradation of school infrastructur e 
has also been planned. In order to improve road connectivity, new bridges and about 578 kms of 
road has been constructed.  To improve tele connectivity in remote areas, satellite phones a nd e-
SATs have been provided. In education and health sectors, 20-beded hospital in Kargil and 3 girls 
hostels with cumulative capacity of 300 beds each have been completed. To improve road 
connectivity within Ladakh, work on Zoji-la Tunnel and Shingo-la tunnel on Himachal side has 
been started.  New airport terminal is being developed at Leh. As certain regions within Ladakh in 
the winter do not have accessibility with the other parts of the UT the approach of the UT 
administration is hundred percent accessibility and mobility within Ladakh. For infrastructure 
development within Ladakh, the UT Administration is contemplating building of multiple tunnels 
between various hill ranges in Ladakh to cut down the distance by using better technology. 
Under-utilization of funds
 
 
5.5.3 The Committee sought to know about the reasons for lower spending of budget in the UT for 
FY 2020-21. The Administration of UT of Ladakh informed the Committee that the reasons for 
savings of funds are as under: - 
(i) Enforcement of lockdown norms due to Covid-19 Pandemic. 
(ii) Working season lost in lockdown and unlock started in off season. 
(iii) Ladakh depends on labour coming from outside. Due to lock down, the execution of 
works was affected. As a result, the responses to the tenders were also affected.  
(iv) Being a new UT, capacity building exercise with relation to employees and implementing 
agencies including strengthening of system is being undertaken in a big way.  
(v) Big-ticket infrastructural projects require comprehensive planning and specialist 
consultants for preparing DPRs. There is also a requirement of designing the projects 
keeping in view energy efficiency and various other aspects related to the region. This 
requires thorough consultation with stakeholders and expert agencies.  
(vi) Various projects have been given to Central Public Sector Undertaking(s) like CPWD, 
NHIDCL and WAPCOS. They took time to establish their offices in the UT of Ladakh for 
planning/execution of projects entrusted to them. 
 
5.5.4 In order to maximize the expenditure, the following steps were taken by UT of Ladakh- 
 
(i) Constitution of requisite Contract Committees and Local Purchase Committees. 
(ii) Engagement of Project Implementing Units (PIUs), Project Management Units (PMUs) 
and Consultants for timely execution of various projects.  
(iii) Training of officers/staff in GeM, PFMS, E-Office etc.  
(iv) Allotment of more than 100 different works in the Financial Year to NHIDCL, NBCC, 
WAPCOS and CPWD. 
(v) Seeking technical assistance from Ministry of Jal Shakti and CPWD for related projects.  
(vi) Engagement of more than 500 staff/field functionaries at subordinate level through 
outsourcing.   
 

84 
 
5.5.5 The Committee sought to know whether there is any impact of unspent balances of budget 
of the current year on the implementation of Special Development Package (SDP). The 
Administration of UT of Ladakh informed the Committee that under SDP, sanction is obtained for 
projects/schemes for which continuous budget support is given by Government of India till the 
completion of such projects/schemes.   
5.5.6 The Committee is disappointed to note that only 27.42% of funds allocated to the UT 
of Ladakh in BE 2020-21 has been spent which is abysmally low. The Committee  is aware 
that due to COVID-19 Pandemic restrictions, the progress in various developmental works 
have been affected. But other UTs (without legislature) in similar situation have utilized more 
than 60% of their allocations. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the MHA may 
take up with the UT Administration to remove bottlenecks and ensure optimal utilization of 
funds in the UT from ensuing year.  The Committee, recommends that the special 
development package (SDP) may be made non-lapsable for few years to cater to the 
developmental need of newly created UT.  
Power
 
 5.5.7 The Committee was informed - as Ladakh is prone to  power shortages, stress is being given 
on the solar and wind power projects accompanied by a smart-grid. The power supply has been 
ensured during the entire winter that the transmission line between Srinagar and Leh is functional. 
Schemes have been proposed to further improve networks, transmission as well as distribution as 
also for augmentation of non-conventional energy in the UT.  Power project at Dah and Hanu, will  
provide 14 MW of electricity.  Steps have also been taken for harnessing solar, hydel and 
geothermal energy.   
Tourism
 
5.5.8 A major proportion of the budget has been allocated for tourism to do brand promotion, 
holding of festivals, etc.  A home-stay policy has been framed to improve home-stays and disburse 
tourism into the rural areas. To develop border areas, especially the Changthang area, a specific 
Changthang package will be implemented, which focuses on development in Changthang.   Priority 
is being given to promote winter tourism including events like ice hockey and convention centres at 
Leh and Kargil. Ladakh Haat for promotion and exhibition of their handlooms and handcrafts has 
also been planned. 
5.5.9 The Committee sought to know about the budgetary provision made for preservation of 
Gompas and Monasteries in Ladakh. The Committee was informed by the Administration of UT of 
Ladakh that a budgetary provision of Rs. 4.00 Cr was provided for preservation of Gompas and 
Monasteries in the budget of UT of Ladakh during the Financial Year 2020-21. 
 
5.5.10 The Committee notes that Rs. 4 crore was allocated in FY 2020-21 for pre servation of 
Gompas and Monasteries in the UT.  The committee recommends that the UT may chalk out 
plan in the ensuing year to utilize the allocated fund for development of old Gompas and 
Monasteries. It will help in conservation of cultural heritage, attract tourists and thereby act  as 
a source of revenue for local people. 
 
Delegation of Power
 
5.5.11 The Committee was further informed by the UT administration of Ladakh that  new 
requisite administrative institutions and infrastructure have been created. The Secretariat has 
become functional with six Secretaries, around 170 officials on deputation and officers from J&K 

85 
 
as middle level administrators in various positions. Ladakh Police has been separated from J&K 
Police.  A separate financial management system has also been operationalized.  
5.5.12 Regarding technical sanctioning powers delegated to the engineering cadre in the UT of 
Ladakh, the Committee was informed that the engineering cadre in UT of Ladakh has been delega ted 
the powers to accord Technical Sanction to the estimates of cost of original works including special 
repairs, renovations, additions etc., as under: - 
a. Committee for Technical Sanction – above Rs. 10 Cr. 
b. Chief Engineer up to Rs.10.00 Cr. 
c. Superintending Engineer up to Rs 3.00 Cr. 
d. Executive Engineers up to Rs 20.00 lakhs 
 
5.5.13 The Committee notes that setting up of administrative institutions and infrastructure  and 
delegation of powers to the engineering cadre have been made in the UT of Ladakh . The 
Committee urges to clearly demarcate role and responsibilities of the institutions for delegat ion 
and division of work and prevent cascading or overlapping of work in the newly created UT. The 
Committee, recommends that the elected representatives should be involved in each  step of 
developmental plans including  policy/programmme formulation, implementation and 
monitoring. The Committee further recommends to increase sanctioning powers delegat ed to the 
engineering cadre and financial power of the Ladakh Autonomous Hill Development Councils. 
 
Investment
 
 
5.5.14 Since the formation of Union Territory of Ladakh, investors from outside have shown 
interest in investing in Ladakh and purchasing land for setting up of ventures in food processing, 
entertainment, education etc,. However, it has not reached the stage of formal proposal. Till date 
after the formation of Union Territory no one has purchased land for setting up of any industria l 
venture. 
 
Connectivity
 
5.5.15 When asked about the arrangements made to ensure round the year air connectivity of 
Ladakh with other places, the Committee was informed by MHA that the commercial flights are 
presently operationalized from KBR airport, Leh to Srinagar, Jammu, Chandigarh and Delhi from 
time to time. Further, the Administration of UT of Ladakh has been operating subsidized helicopter 
service i. e. B3 & MI-172 Helicopters operated by Pawan Hans Ltd. to connect various far -flung 
areas within and outside Ladakh i.e. Srinagar to Kargil and Leh. The helicopter service is demand 
based. 
 
Vacancies in different cadres of UT of Ladakh
 
 
 5.5.16 The vacancies for Government jobs in the newly created Union Territory of Ladakh are based 
on the apportionment of employees between the UT of J&K and UT of Ladakh in accordance with the 
provisions of section 89(2) of the Jammu & Kashmir Reorganisation Act, 2019.Out of the above 
vacancies, the Gazetted posts are to be filled by the Union Public Service Commission and the Non-
gazetted District Cadre posts are to be filled by the competent authorities of the UT. A total of 493 
District Cadre Posts have been filled through the respective Ladakh Autonomous Hill Development 
Council Subordinate Services Recruitment Board(s) of Leh and Kargil during the calendar year 2020. 
Further, a total of 538 persons have also been engaged on an outsourcing basis. 
 
 

86

Transport 
 
 5.6.6 The Committee was informed by the administration of UT of Lakshadweep that as the islands of 
the UT are scattered in Arabian Sea and isolated from mainland, shipping sector is the lifeline of 
islands.  Allocation under Transport Sector is mainly for acquisition of new ships, running & 
maintenance of shipping services, augmentation of berthing & ship to shore services, maintenance of 
helicopter and flight services and helipads/ Airports.  Allocation for Transport sector in RE 2019-20 is 
Rs.492.35 crore, whereas BE 2020-21 is Rs.548.20 crore i.e. an increased allocation of Rs.55.85 crore 
after adjusting the decrease/ increase in each programme/ activity  The enhanced allocation is mainly for

5.6.7 As islands are scattered in the Arabian Sea and isolated from the mainland, shipping sector 
is the lifeline of the islands. Out of total allocation of Rs.1,440.56 crore, 38% i.e. Rs.548.20 crore is 
exclusively for Transport sector (Port, Shipping and Aviation).  UTL Administration has already 
been operating passenger and cargo ships as well as High Speed Vessels for ensuring inter-isl and 
and mainland-island marine connectivity.  Acquisition of ships and vessels are in accordance to the 
15 years Perspective Plan 2015-2030, which is already approved by the Ministry. 
5.6.8 The Ministry of Civil Aviation has been requested to expand the connectivity across the 
islands, i.e., Agatti, Minicoy and Kavaratti, under UDAN Scheme and they have requested for 
seaplane service also across the island.  Further, a big Navy airport is coming up in Minicoy Island 
on the southern tip of Lakshadweep which  will transform the entire economy of that region.  The 
airstrip of the Agatti is being expanded for use of large planes to increase the carrying capacity of 
the planes which is about 60-70  persons per flight right now. The Airports Authority of India has 
also been asked to make feasibility study so that the Airbus 320 can also land at Agatti island. The 
Committee sought to know whether the UT of Lakshadweep  has helicopter services. The 
representative of UT of Lakshdweep replied that three Pawan Hans helicopters are being used  for 
medical evacuation only and till now no private operators have come in the UT for heli-tourism. 
5.6.9 The Committee is of the view that turboprops aircrafts with 40-70 seating capacity 
require very short take-off and landing strips and are economically viable. As such, they ar e 
ideally suited for the islands and there is no need to land heavy aircrafts and airbuses in the 
Islands.  Therefore, the focus should be on optimal use of the small aircrafts with STOL 
(short takeoff and landing) to promote tourism.  The Committee recommends to revisit the 
proposal for extension of runway for landing Airbus as it is not economically viable and in the 
ecologically sensitive areas it may not be advisable. The Committee also recommend s to take 
appropriate steps for attracting private operators for heli-tourism as it will help tourist  to 
move swiftly from one island to other and also enjoy the aerial view of beautiful land scape of 
the islands. 
 

90 
 
Monitoring of Shipping/Harbor Development Works 
5.6.10 The Committee was informed by the representative of MHA that the Government 
constituted the Island Development Agency (IDA) 2015 for the development of UTs of 
Lakshadweep and Andaman & Nicobar Islands and a number of meetings headed by the Union 
Home Minister are held every year.  The development trajectory of both these groups of islands has  
been put on a completely different pedestal subsequent to the constitution of IDA
.  
5.6.11 The Committee was informed by the Administration of UT of Lakshdweep, that considering 
the importance of development of harbor infrastructure in Lakshadweep islands and to achieve 
100% project target upon commissioning, it has become necessary to incorporate marine and 
shipping expertise at every stage of the project, such as identification of site, model studies, 
preparation of DPR and implementation etc. It has also become necessary to have a separate  
establishment for Lakshadweep headed by a Chief Engineer with complete administrative and 
financial powers for implementation of harbor development projects and timely maintenance o f 
harbor infrastructure. 
5.6.12 The Ministry of Ports, Shipping and Waterways has been requested to establish a separate 
Division of Andaman and Lakshadweep Harbour Works (ALHW), a subordinate organization of 
Ministry of Ports, Shipping and Waterways, to be headed by a Chief Engineer with complete 
administrative and financial powers and also involvement of Marine and Shipping 
expertise/consultancy for gaining efficiency in harbor development and maintenance in 
Lakshadweep.  The Ministry of Ports, Shipping and Waterways has constituted a Committee  to 
decide the modalities for distribution of manpower, budget and other issues likely to arise in case of 
bifurcation of ALHW.  Based on recommendations of the Committee, further action on 
restructuring of ALHW will be considered by Ministry of Ports, Shipping and Waterways. 
5.6.13 The Committee is of the view that the passenger ship is the lifeline of the people of UT of 
Lakshdweep as connectivity to the island is very difficult. But, the Committee notes that there is no 
embarkation or disembarkation facility during night and passengers have to sleep in boats till 
morning.  The reason is the limited infrastructure.  There are six Islands out of which eastern side 
jetties have been made for four Islands  The reason for this persistent problem is  the absence of  
charge of Harbour Works Authority to the UT of Lakshadweep due to which, proper monitoring is 
not being done and infrastructure work is being  delayed for years.   
5.6.14 The Committee notes that the Administration of the UT of Lakshadweep does not 
have any control over the implementing agency of the Harbour Development. The Com mittee 
is of the view that that a separate Division of Andaman and Lakshadweep Harbour Works 
(ALHW) should be established for gaining efficiency in Harbour Development and 
maintenance in Lakshadweep. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the MHA may 
delegate control of the project to the Administration of the UT of Lakshadweep on a priority  
basis. This will ensure the role of UT Administrator in discharge of its duties and justify 
proper utilisation of funds allocated to the UT.

5.6.16 Lakshadweep is a group of tiny islands scattered in the Arabian Sea.    The size of the 
islands vary from 0.10 to 4.90 Sq.km.  These are also coral islands susceptible to damage, if not 
developed properly.  Due to the size of the islands and nature of the ecology, very limited number  
of industries can be developed in the islands.  Few of them are tourism, fisheries and coconut based 
industries.  Due to its natural beauty of the lagoons and deep sea, potential for tourism is almost 
infinite. The tourism industry can generate meaningful employment to the local populace without 
damaging the nature.  Therefore sustainable tourism development is a necessity for the islands. 
5.6.17 On the tourism development, a NITI Aayog led model is being followed where carrying 
capacity analysis is being done for each of the islands for sustainable development.  Detailed 
studies have been carried out about the carrying capacity of each island from the ecological 
perspective and bidding out water villas in Lakshadweep it is in the advanced process on the lines 
of Maldives
.   
5.6.18 Based on the ecological and cultural profile of Lakshadweep islands, Lakshadweep 
Administration has formulated an Integrated Island Management Plan and carrying capacity based 
on the land, water and resources have been worked out.   
 
Internet connectivity
 
5.6.19 Broadband has been a very major issue in the  islands of Lakshadweep which restricts their 
development Therefore, the Government of India has announced undersea cable connectivity for 
Lakshadweep to provide unlimited broadband connectivity, for which the bidding process is 
underway and the project will be completed by 2022. 

92 
 
5.6.20 The Committee notes the undersea cable connectivity project for the UT of 
Lakshadweep to provide unlimited broadband connectivity which is proposed to be 
completed by 2022. Due to COVID-19 Pandemic, education has been shifted to onlin e mode 
which requires good internet connectivity to attend online classes and watch videos. Further, 
internet facility is also very important in the UT for internal security purpose given its 
proximity to Maldives. The Committee, therefore, recommends that till the completion of 
under sea cable project, interim arrangements should be made for providing  internet facility 
in the UT so that the students may continue with their online education and internal security 
measures remain intact.

5.6.22 Regarding development of renewable energy, the Committee was informed that the 
Lakshadweep Administration has signed MoU with Solar Energy Corporation of India (SECI) on 
7th October 2018 for the deployment of Solar Power projects in different islands of Lakshadweep 
for setting up of 25.14 MW Solar Power projects with up to 75.71 MWH BESS in 11 islands. Asian 
Development Bank (ADB) has been tasked by Govt. of India, Ministry of New and Renewable 
Energy (MNRE) to provide the support in Lakshadweep for creating an enabling and responsive 
environment for uptake of solar roof top installations at scale.  
5.6.23 The Committee observes that currently, the power generation in the UT is diesel 
based.  The spillage of diesel stored in power houses directly goes to the groundwater an d 
affects the people living around. Therefore, it is important to shift from diesel base power 
generation to renewable power generation. Considering the small size of the UT, it can b e 
fully powered through green energy.  Therefore, the Committee recommend s that the 

93 
 
Administration of UT of Lakshdweep may speed up the ongoing projects for developmen t of 
renewable energy in the UT also make plans for solar rooftops, solar panels on lagoons, etc. 
Fisheries 
 
5.6.24 The  total fisheries potential of UTL has been estimated at One Lakh metric tons by Central 
Marine Fishries Research Institute (CMFRI). However, the present exploitation /fish landing is 20-
25% of potential resources. To cope up with the changing standards of fishing industry and to 
exploit the deep sea resources, size of the crafts needs to be increased and the facilities on board are 
to be enhanced to carry out multiple day fishing operations. Proposal is to extend financial 
assistance for introduction of larger deep sea fishing vessels, up grading the existing crafts and to 
supply latest fish finding and navigation equipment for use on board the fishing vessels. The UT 
administration is implementing a Scheme “Strengthening of Fishing Fleets & Improvement of 
Crafts”. Under this scheme, department provides financial assistance of 40%  to the local poor 
fishermen  for the construction of  bigger type multi day pole & line/long line  fishing   vessels, 
medium type pole & line fishing  boats etc.  
 
5.6.25 Besides, encouragement is being provided to   fishermen for setting up of hatcheries and 
production of ornamentals under controlled conditions. The research institutes have developed 
technologies for the hatchery and grow-out of clown and damsel fishes. A sea weed cultivation 
project has already been initiated with coordination with CMFRI at Rs. 49 Lakhs earmarked for 
2020-21. 
 
5.6.26 For Development of Post-Harvest Fish Processing Facilities & Marketing, UTLA has 
proposed to create shore based infrastructural facilities like ice plants, cold storages etc. for the 
utilization of fish waste, a fish meal plant and production of value added fishery products. An 
integrated fish processing unit is also proposed and Rs. 35 Lakhs is earmarked for the purpose. In 
addition to this, an amount of Rs. 1 crore is earmarked  under capital head for purchase of 
equipment & machinery for Boat Building Yard, Workshop & slip way/ Ice Plant & Fish market 
etc.   
 
5.6.27 Marketing is the main issue faced by local fishermen. The Department of Fisheries is 
promoting marketing of fresh fish by facilitating bigger type collecting vessels for colle ction & 
transportation of fish from islands to mainland. Further, UTL Administration has taken a policy 
decision to open up the market to Marine Products Export Development Authority (MPEDA) 
registered exporters to collect and export Tuna and Tuna like fishes from island to mainland. For 
Sashmi Sea food, one of the MPEDA registered exporters has already set up a packing/ processing 
unit at Agatti. Moreover, the Dept. of Fisheries, UTL has signed a MoU with Kerala State Coastal 
Area Development Corporation (KSCADC) for handholding  in post harvest and marketing of 
fishes especially Tuna. The draft agreement with MPEDA for the purpose of technical 
collaboration for exporting of Tuna and other fisheries products has also been finalized and the 
same will be signed within one month. 
 
5.6.28 The Committee observes that the fishing potential of the UT of Lakshdweep is n ot 
being harnessed properly. The fishermen are not catching enough fish  due  to lack of 
infrastructure like small crafts, ice plant, cold storage, processing units, etc. to catch, preserve 
and process fishes before they are sold in the market. The Committee is of the view that  
proper storage and processing unit will give fishermen the confidence that if the c atch is not 
sold, at least it can be preserved, and can be sold in the market the next day. T he Committee, 
therefore, recommends that investments should be made for creation of basic inf rastructure 
to boost fishing industry in the UT to provide income security to the fishermen. The 

94 
 
Committee is of the view that inland fisheries too have vast scope for the development of 
fisheries sector in the UT. The Committee, therefore,  recommends to utilize the avail able 
inland resources like ponds, swamps and low lying rain fed areas,  for fresh  water fish 
farming through mariculture, cage culture and sea weed culture The Committee also 
recommends to increase budgetary allocation to develop fishery industry in the UT. 
 
Smart City Project at Kavaratti
 
 
5.6.29 Total project costs sanctioned under Kavaratti Smart City is Rs.526.61 crore.  Out of this, 
projects costing Rs.346.50 crore are to be funded directly by Kavaratti Smart City Limited (KSC L) 
and remaining projects are proposed as convergence projects i.e funds will flow either from 
concerned central Ministry or UT Administration department.  Out of KSCL funding of Rs.346.50 
crore, Ministry will bear 50% i.e. Rs.173.25 crore and equal amount is to be released by UTLA to 
KSCL.  The Ministry has already released Rs.60 crore to KSCL.  UTLA earmarked an amount of 
Rs.1 crore only in BE 2020-21, which has already been released, and projected additionality of 
Rs.59 crore in RE stage, but this was not agreed to. 
 
5.6.30 The Committee notes that Kavaratti island has been declared as Smart City P roject. 
The Committee further notes that some part of the project will be funded  directly by 
Kavaratti Smart City Limited (KSCL) and remaining projects will be funded either from 
concerned Central Ministry or UT Administration Department. Due to funding of projec t 
from multiple sources, the progress of work has been very slow. The Committee, ther efore, 
recommends that the MHA may take up the matter with Ministry of Urban Development and 
UT Administration to bring clarity on funding of the project. A decision may also be taken 
that which part of the project will be funded by whom so that the projects that are being 
delayed due to non-availability of funds may take off at the earliest.

5.7.4 The Updated status on Water Treatment Plant (WTP) project at Wazirabad and Landfill sites 
at Bhalswa and Ghazipur is as follows:- 
 
1. Water Treatment Plant (WTP) project at Wazirabad
 
 
Delhi Jal Board took up the project of Improvement of Water Supply Services in Wazirabad 
Water Treatment Plant (WTP) command areas with financial assistance from Asian Development Bank 
(ADB). Delhi Water Supply Improvement Investment Program (DWSIIP) proposed to improve the 
infrastructure, management and performance of the water supply services in the proposed Wazirabad 
WTP command area located in North Delhi. The total cost of the project was Rs. 2,243 crore (USD 415 
million). The project cost was proposed to be funded through loan from ADB to the tune of 70% (USD 
290 million) and balance 30% cost to be borne by GNCTD. Subsequently ADB has withdrawn this 
project. The project is no longer included in the country operations business plan of ADB for India 
2021-2023, which was endorsed by the Department of Economic Affairs.  
 
2. Landfill site at Bhalswa
 
 
  North Delhi Municipal Corporation is maintaining a Sanitary Land Fill (SLF) near Mukarba 
Chowk at Bhalswa, G.T. Karnal Road. This SLF site is spread over an area of about 70 acres of land  
and receives about 2,200 MT of fresh Municipal Solid Waste (MSW) daily. At present 21 trommels are 
installed out of which 15 trommels are functional. About 4000 to 4200 MT of legacy waste is being 
processed daily at Bhalswa dumpsite. About 10 lakh MT of legacy waste has been cleared from 
Bhalswa SLF.  
 
  Timeline to clear Legacy Waste of about 70 lakh MT out of 80 lakh MT is as follows: 
 
(i) 25% of the legacy waste will be processed till April 2021. 
(ii) 50% of the legacy waste will be processed till October 2021. 
(iii) 100%  of the legacy waste will be processed till June 2022. 
(iv) The legacy waste as per above action plan will materialize subject to the availability 
of suitable land for disposal of inert material. 
(v) Request letters were sent to the NHAI, NTPC, DDA & other agencies, requesting 
them to utilize inert material in their works. In response, only NTPC has permitted 
dumping of inert material at their Eco Park site, Badarpur. 
(vi) The inert material is being disposed at low lying areas near Bhalswa dump site, at 
ECO Park of NTPC Badarpur, M.C. Pry. School Mukundpur, municipal parks at 
Bhalswa Dairy and Rani Khera. More low lying areas are being explored for disposal 
of inert materials. 
(vii) First mound of 11 m height in an area of 14.90 acre has been completely 
cleared.Portions of second mound of 12 m height in an area of 1.5 acre towards 
Shradhanand Colony and 3 m height in an area of 21.50 acre towards Outer Ring 
Road have also been cleared. Efforts are being made to increase the number of 
trammels to further reduce the height of dump.  
 
3. Landfill site at Ghazipur
  
 
  East Delhi Municipal Corporation is maintaining a Sanitary Land Fill (SLF) near NH-24. This 
SLF site is spread in about 70 acre of land. About 2,000-2,200 MT of fresh MSW is received daily and 
about 3,000 MT (as an average) of legacy waste is being processed daily. About 3.00 lakh MT of legacy 
waste has been processed at SLF Ghazipur dumpsite. 

98 
 
 
  Time line to clear legacy waste of 140 lakh MT is as follows: 
 
(i) 25% of legacy waste will be processed by March-2022. 
(ii) 50% of legacy waste will be processed by March-2023. 
(iii) 75% of legacy waste will be processed by March-2024. 
(iv) 100% of legacy waste will be processed by December-2024. 
 
East Delhi Municipal Corporation (EDMC) is making all efforts to meet the above 
mentioned timeline but there are some constraints as below: 
 
(i) As the garbage at SLF site Ghazipur has attained the height of about 65 mtrs. and since 
no space is available at ground level, EDMC has started creating space above the 
garbage at certain locations for installation of trommels. The trommels are being 
installed after clearing/ processing a stretch of legacy waste and constructing platform 
over the legacy waste. In this way, EDMC has installed 15 nos. trommels of 300 TPD 
each at SLF site Ghazipur. 
(ii) At SLF site Ghazipur, no space is available for stacking of segregated material. 
(iii) Monsoon period and Winter Season reduces the efficiency of processing the legacy 
waste. 
(iv) Practical problems like break down of machines/ power supply also hampers the 
progress. 
(v) EDMC is densely populated and there is no space for filling of this soil like inert 
material in such a huge quantity. However EDMC is making all out efforts with other 
agencies and has also signed a MoU with NHAI to use this soil like material in their 
road construction in 2016. But till date, no headway has been achieved. Earlier EDMC 
had proposed to DDA to construct a missing portion of bund between ISBT to 
Wazirabad Road on Eastern Bank of Yamuna River. In response, DDA has informed 
that this matter will be taken up with PWD & I&FC department of GNCTD. Matter has 
been taken up with these departments for construction of bund on priority. 
(vi) Disposal of RDF matter is being taken up with cement industries as per the RDF 
utilization policy of Govt. of India. 
(vii) About 12 mtrs. height in certain stretches of dump has been reduced by biomining and 
efforts are being made to increase the number of trommels to further reduce the height 
of dump. 
 
5.7.5 The Committee expresses its concern about the landfill sites at Bhalsawa and Gazipu r, 
which have been repeatedly expressed over the years.  The Committee observes that these 
sites continue to smoulder from time to time and releases toxic gases. Apart from stubble 
burning, landfill sites are also one of the factors for pollution of the air and the poor air 
quality in the National Capital.  The Committee recommends to increase the number of 
trommels to further reduce the height of dump at the landfill site of Bhalsawa and  Ghazipur 
and allocate adequate funds for the same.  
 
5.7.6 The Committee also notes that EDMC has signed a MoU with NHAI to use soil like 
material in their road construction in 2016. But no progress has been made so far. T he 
Committee, therefore, recommends that the MHA may take up the matter with NHAI on 
priority for making arrangements to use the waste materials for road construction as EDMC 

99 
 
is densely populated and in absence of filling of this soil like inert mate rial in such a huge 
quantity, pollution will become more severe in the adjoining areas. 
 
5.8 Demand No. 57 - Transfers to Jammu & Kashmir 
 
5.8.1 An amount of Rs. 30,757 crore has been allotted to the UT of J&K in BE 2021-22. The 
Committee sought to know the Administration‟s plans to utilize the allocation Rs. 30,757 crore in 
the upcoming fiscal year. The Administrator of the UT replied that the amount is in addition to the 
resources raised by the UT and will be spent on various items including 7 th CPC allowances and 
LTC, for meeting the salary, pension, gratuity etc., for disaster management projects and financing  
of projects implemented by rural and urban local bodies and for financing the projects and schemes 
of UT of J&K.   Additional requirements, if any, will be reviewed at RE stage and projected 
accordingly.  
 
5.8.2 The Committee sought to know about the quantum of revenue generated by UT of J&K by 
their own resources. The Administration of the UT of Jammu & Kashmir replied that  out of a total 
budgetary outlay of Rs. 1,08,621 crore (2021-22), tax revenue of J&K is projected as Rs. 16,276 
crore and own non-tax revenue as Rs. 8,209 crore, totaling Rs. 24,485 crore for the year 2021-22. 
Further, out of total budgetary outlay of Rs.92,341 crore under RE 2020-21, own tax revenue is 
projected as Rs. 10837 crore and non-tax revenue as Rs. 6,193 crore, totaling to Rs. 17,030 Crore.  
 
5.8.3 The Committee was informed that orders relating to adaptation of 48 Central Laws and 167 
State Laws in respect of UT of J&K have been notified.  Similarly, Orders relating to adaptation of 
44 Central Laws and 148 State Laws in respect of UT of Ladakh have been notified. Further, the  
Jammu and Kashmir Official Languages Act, 2020 has been notified on 27 th September, 2020.  
Kashmiri, Dogri, Urdu, Hindi and English have become official Languages of UT of J&K. 
 
5.8.4 Regarding the distribution of administrative/financial powers among the local bodies, 
Panchayats, BDC, DDC and UT administration to avoid the conflict, the Administration of the UT 
of Jammu & Kashmir replied that the Panchayati Raj Act of J&K was modified in 2018 and 2020 
to  enable transferring of funds, functions and functionaries to the three-tier Panchayati Raj System. 
Departments like School Education, Health, ICDS, Rural Development, Social Welfare and others 
have been transferred to Panchayat Raj Institutions at various levels. The Finance Commission 
grants are also transferred to the Panchayati Raj Institutions for their empowerment. 
 
5.8.5 The Committee enquired that by when Assembly election will be conducted in UT of J&K. 
The decision to conduct the Assembly election in J&K rests with the Election Commission of India 
after the process of delimitation is completed by the Delimitation Commission.  
 
5.8.6 The Committee notes that School Education, Health, ICDS, Rural Development, Social 
Welfare and other Departments have been transferred to Panchayat Raj Institu tions at 
various levels along with financial powers.  The Committee is of the view that adequate 
delegation of power should be made for the grassroots institutions of Panchayats. Therefore , 
the Committee recommends to devolve more funds, functions and functionaries to the 
Panchayats in the UT to bring it at par with Panchayats of the other States/UTs.  
5.8.7 On the equitable development of the UT of J&K, the Committee was informed that earlier 
West Pakistan refugees  could vote in the Parliament elections, but had no local domicile rights. 
They have been given domiciles now.   The women who married out of their State, their children 
would lose all their rights.  Now, that has been restored. The Scheduled Tribes in Jammu and 
Kashmir did not have political representation but the district panchayats which have just been 

100 
 
elected have 10 per cent representation for STs in panchayats.   Anantnag, Poonch and Rajouri, all 
are reserved for STs.  'Pahari'  people, who constitute 7-8 per cent of the population, have been 
provided reservation.  Further, domicile rules have been framed,which enables Government jobs to 
be reserved, to a certain extent, to domiciles.  Although it is not very rigid, but by and large, it has 
assuaged the feelings of the people.   
 
Air Connectivity 
 
 
5.8.8 To improve air connectivity, Srinagar airport was declared as International Airport and Air 
India had started flights from Srinagar to Dubai. The operation was stopped later by Air India 
quoting commercial non-availability.The Government of J&K has taken up the matter of operating 
International flights from Srinagar with the Ministry of Civil Aviation, seeking their intervention to 
impress on various private airlines to undertake International operations of commercial flights from 
Srinagar.  
 
5.8.9 The Committee notes that Srinagar has an International Airport, but currently, no 
international commercial flights are operating from the airport. The Committee beli eves that 
in absence of direct international flights to Sringar, the real tourism potential of the UT may 
not be realized to its full potential as international tourists have to take connecting flights to 
reach Srinagar which is expensive and time consuming. Therefore, the Committee 
recommends that the MHA may take up the matter with Ministry of Civil Aviation and take 
appropriate steps to remove bottlenecks and commence international commercial fli ghts 
operations from Srinagar Airport at the earliest. 
 
Tourism
 
 
5.8.10 The COVID-19 had a considerable impact on the tourism trade and travel industry as the 
tourist flowing down to J&K declined. The fall in the tourist inflow had adversely affected the 
livelihood of the stakeholders viz hoteliers, guest house owners, tour & travel operators, taxi 
operators, etc. The Government of J&K announced a relief package of Rs. 11.67 crore to the 
workers such as Shikarawalas, Ponywallas, Tourist guides, Sledge wallas etc, working in this 
industry. However, the Govt. of J&K gradually opened the sector under strict covid protocol and 
the sector is now witnessing gradual improvement in tourist footfall. 
 
5.8.11 The Committee expressed its concern that some tourist in J&K face problems from local 
taxi operators who insist to take taxies operated by their known persons at exorbitant rates for 
onward journey after reaching a certain point. The Administration of the UT of Jammu & Kashmir 
replied that while there is no prohibition of taxies from outside J&K with valid permits being 
allowed in J&K, it has come to notice that in some tourist areas like Gulmarg, Pahalgam and 
Sonmarg, there is a system by which local sightseeing is being done by local taxi operators only. 
This apparently works by mutual consent and same arrangement is in Shimla, Manali and other 
tourist places. The Government will look into the matter and see that tourists are not 
inconvenienced. 
 
5.8.12  The Committee notes that in some tourist areas like Gulmarg, Pahalgam and 
Sonmarg, local sightseeing is being done by local taxi operators only.  The Committee 
observes that at times, tourists are being charged exorbitant rates by local taxi operators in 
these places. Therefore, the Committee recommends that the Administration of UT of J&K 

101 
 
may issue tariffs for hiring taxis to visit tourist places and put a ceiling on maximum rate that 
can be charged from customer. 
Vacancies
 
 
5.8.13 To oversee the recruitment and to have a single nodal point, the Government of J&K 
constituted an Accelerated Recruitment Committee (ARC) with the aim to identify vacancies across 
various departments which could be filled up on immediate basis. The Committee in the Phase- I 
identified 10,000 Class-IV vacancies across various departments, out of which 8,575 have already 
been put to advertisement by the Board under the provisions of Jammu and Kashmir, Appointment 
to Class-IV (Special Recruitment) Rules, 2020. As part of Phase-II of Accelerated Recruitments 
Drive, the Committee has identified 12,379 Gazetted and Non-Gazetted vacancies which comprises 
of 533 Gazetted and 11,846 Non-Gazetted vacancies. Out of these, 383 Gazetted and 7,433 Non-
Gazetted vacancies have already been referred to the recruiting agencies. 
 
Infrastructure
 
5.8.14 On the infrastructure front, the Prime Minister‟s Development Programme, PMDP is being 
implemented for the UT.    Three years ago, the expenditure was about 20 per cent, which has now 
increased to about 60 per cent.  The funds are being used for roads, railway lines, power projects, 
infrastructure in tourism, health, education, etc.  J&K is likely to get connected by the railway  line  
by 15 th of August 2022 and the dream of a train journey from Kashmir to Kanyakumari will 
become a reality.
     The Committee was further informed that the tourism sector has recovered well 
in the UT since December. 2020.  The UT Administration has taken steps to promote adventure 
tourism, golf tourism and religious circuits. 
5.8.15 The Committee was further informed that the power sector has immense potential of almost 
27,000 MW in the UT.  But, till now, about 3,500 MW of power has been generated, 3,500 MW 
projects are under progress and, addition of another 3,500 MW is in the pipeline.    The Chenab 
River has potential of 16,000 MW.  The UT has also witnessed good power supply in winters. 
 
5.8.16 The Committee is aware of the unregulated blasting of dynamite being done in 
Himalayan region for major projects. The Committee is of the view that it is important to 
ensure that the developmental projects are not disturbing the nature and people living in 
these areas. The Committee strongly recommends for forbidding the use of dynamite s in 
Himalayan region. The Committee reiterates its recommendation given at para 3.8.11 of 
chapter III. 
Health and Education
 
5.8.17 Regarding development of health sector, the Committee was informed that the Ayushman 
Bharat, PMJAY, has been universalised in Jammu & Kashmir and everyone is receiving its 
benefits. Further, five new medical colleges have been opened in last 5 years.    
5.8.18 On the education side, the Committee was informed that the primary education is very good 
in the UT, but there are not enough colleges.   The number of colleges have been increased from  
100 to 150 and more higher institutions are coming up.  
5.8.19 The Committee enquired about the proposals for opening of Private Schools and Private 
hospitals in UT of J&K. The Administration of the UT of Jammu & Kashmir replied that owing to 
restrictions of ownership of land, a very few of the private national hospital chains have established 

102 
 
hospitals in J&K. However, following the relaxation in land ownership for Industries and Service 
Sector and the Government‟s efforts to promote private hospitals, the J&K Government has 
received proposals for establishing 18 private hospitals with investment of Rs. 3,200 crore in the 
form of MoUs. Similarly, J&K Government has received 33 proposals with an investment of Rs. 
2,100 crore in the education sector.  
 
5.8.20 The Committee sought to know whether 4G internet services have been operational in all 
parts of Jammu & Kashmir. The MHA replied that the restrictions on mobile data services and the 
restrictions on mobile data services and fixed line internet connectivity have been revoked in all 
parts of J&K by Home Department UT of J&K Government with effect from 05th February, 2021.   
 
5.8.21 The Committee notes that internet facility was not available in the UT of J&K and has 
been restored w.e.f. 5 th February, 2021. The Committee observes that the schools have 
remained shut for a long time due to COVID-19 Pandemic. The education was shif ted from 
offline to online mode. But, in absence of internet in the UT, even the students having laptops/ 
mobile phones were unable to access online classes.  The Committee is of the view that  
internet plays a crucial role in ensuring that the flow of education remains u ndisturbed and 
the process of learning does not stop even during a Pandemic. The Committee, there fore, 
recommends that the MHA may consult with the Ministries of Education, Electronics & 
Information Technology and other concerned agency is to prepare a blueprint to protect the 
right to education of children during a Pandemic or any other extraordinar y circumstances 
with frequent internet shutdowns. 
 
 
Agricultural and Industrial Development
 
 
5.8.22 For the development of agriculture and industry, the  representative of the UT informed the 
Committee that the agricultural land is being protected for agriculturists of Jammu and Kashmir on 
similar lines as Uttarakhand and Himachal.  However, the land has been opened up for industries. 
This has been one of the reasons why J&K, unlike Himachal and Uttarakhand, has not benefited 
from any industrial package in the past because till one has title to a land, industry cannot be set up.  
J&K produces about 85 per cent of the countries' apples, hundred per cent of the walnuts and then 
innumerable fruit crops, etc.  The UT Administration if planning to triple the apple production in 
the next five years.  Steps are also being taken in Jammu 'Basmati rice' and other horticulture 
products.  
5.8.23 The Committee was informed by the MHA that Jammu and Kashmir Trade Promotion 
Organization has been designated as the Investment Promotion Agency (IPA) for the UT of J&K 
with the mandate to coordinate activities for increasing investments in various sectors in J&K. 
Further, a Land Bank of 6,000 acres is contemplated to be developed for providing ample land for 
industry to establish, out of which 3,000 acres of land have already been identified. The Committee 
enquired about the number of proposals of investment received in Union Territories of Jammu & 
Kashmir since its creation and purchase of land by people from outside the State. The Committee 
was informed by the UT of J&K that 456 MoUs have been signed amounting to Rs. 23,152.17 
crore in the UT. Further, no person from outside has purchased land in J&K, since  the Industrial 
land allotment policy is in formulation and land allotment has not commenced. 
  
5.8.24 The Committee sought to know about the number of industries that have come up in UT of 
J&K and whether domicile certificate is a prerequisite for establishment of a Industries. The 
Administration of the UT of Jammu & Kashmir replied that the new J&K Industrial Development 
scheme has recently been approved by GoI. The Government has made all preparations for 

103 
 
capitalizing on this scheme by developing new Industrial estates. The Government intends to hold 
promotional events to promote the policy among investors. Land is being provided in the new 
industrial estates. Investors are also free to obtain land on their own. There is no domicile 
requirement for the establishment of industries. The allocations made/proposed to be made for for 
key productive sectors in UT of J&K are as under- 
 
(i) Agriculture Sector: Rs 2,331.43 crore for 2020-21 and Rs. 2,950.52 crore in 
2021-22. 
(ii) Horticulture Sector: Rs 517.61 crore for 2020-21 and Rs. 588.88 crore in 2021-
22. 
(iii) Industries Sector: Rs 727.58 crore in 2020-21 and Rs. 1,091.72 crore in 2021-22. 
(iv) Animal/Sheep Husbandry Sector: Rs 766.75 crore in 2020-21 and Rs. 911.71 
crore in 2021-22. 
(v) Fisheries sector: Rs 210.91 crore in 2020-21 and Rs. 215.86 crore in 2021-22. 
 
5.8.25 To provide exemption to handloom, handicraft and other sectors in J&K from GST, the 
Committee was informed by the MHA that the Government of J&K has taken up the matter with 
the GST Council, which has informed that the issue of exemption to handloom and handicraft 
sectors has been taken up with the Fitment Committee for consideration. The case is pending for 
consideration in the Fitment Committee.

the MHA may take up with the Ministry of Finance for enhanced allocation for UT at RE 
stage. 
 
Coastal erosion
 
 
5.9.5 The pristine coastline of Puducherry, which is the lifeline of this small coastal town, has 
faced significant erosion over the last three decades. The erosion of the coastline in Puducherry is 
due to natural as well as anthropogenic activities. In particular, erosion was accelerated after the 
construction of a port in 1989. The beach of Puducherry town has been completely lost. There were  
continuous demands from the public and civil society to restore the lost beach. Accordingly, 
restoration of the beach and erosion control has been one of the top priorities of the Government of 
Puducherry and the Government has taken several measures to address the issue. 
 
5.9.6 The DST&E approached Institute of Ocean Management, Anna University and National 
Institute of Ocean Technology (NIOT), Chennai to conduct a scientific study to protect and nourish 
the coast of Puducherry and the adjoining areas by adopting site specific coastal protection and 
restoration techniques. NIOT monitored the coastal processes responsible for shoreline changes 
from 2012 and has prepared a comprehensive Shoreline Management Plan for Puducherry in May 
2015. Subsequently, based on the detailed hydrodynamic and coastal process studies carried out, 
NIOT has designed and implemented the project. The proposed solution involves- 
 
(i) Construction of one near shore Wedge reef opposite to the Chief Secretariat on the north 
end of Pondicherry town foreshore, with the crest at Chart Datum. 
(ii) Construction of one offshore reef placed at the south end, 300 m north of the pier, with 
the crest at 1 m above Chart Datum. 
(iii) Sand nourishment using 4,50,000 m 3 of sand between northern and southern reef along 
the coastline of Pondicherry Town near Gandhi Statue.   
 
5.9.7 The construction of Near Shore Wedge Reef was commenced in March 2017 by NIOT a nd 
completed in August 2018. The Government of Puducherry is carrying out necessary sand 
nourishment in consultation with NIOT to supplement the beach formation. The offshore 
submerged reef proposed under the project is yet to be constructed. The Government of Puducherry 
has requested the Ministry of Earth Sciences to take up the construction of offshore submerged reef 
also through NIOT, since the project is a first of its kind in the country and has been completely 
designed and implemented by NIOT. If the Central Government funds this project through suitable 
scheme, it would be a significant contribution towards arresting coastal erosion as envisaged by 
NIOT. 

105 
 
5.9.8 The Committee notes that the beach of Puducherry town has been com pletely lost due 
to erosion. The Committee also notes that Government of Puducherry has taken several 
measures to address the issue. The Committee, recommends, that adequate budgetary 
allocation may be made for the same and the beach may be restored to within a stipulated 
time frame. The Committee would like to be apprised about the progress made in this regard. 
 
Land issues with Tamil Nadu for Common Airport 
 
5.9.9. The Committee was  informed by the UT of Pudducherry that about 2 lakh tourists visit UT 
every year  and last year the  number of international tourists was around 60,000.The Committee 
was further informed that the runway of the airport in Puducherry is about 1,600 metres long in 
which small aircrafts land. But the UT administration desires to expand it to increase the arrival of 
tourists. Further, the UT will have to acquire land from Tamil Nadu to expand the airport. 
5.9.10 A MoU was signed between the Gove rnment of Puducherry and AAI on 16 th  June, 2007 
for expansion of Puducherry Airport in two P hases. Pha se-I involved extension o f the existing 
runway by 260 meters to en able landing of AT R/Bombardier type of air craft alongwith ground 
support facilities. Land to an ext ent of 19.92 Hectares in Puducherry area was acquired at a cost o f 
Rs.18.95 crore and h anded over to Airports Authori ty o f India on 25 th July, 2007 fo r first phase 
development.   AAI has completed the extension of the runway. Phase-II comprises extension of an 
additional 1000 meters of runway to fa cilitate operation of large jet aircrafts b y acquiring  another 
85 He ctares o f adjacent land in the State of Tamil Nadu.  The T amil Nadu administration wa s 
requested on 3rd June, 2007 to acquire 200 acres (85.56 hectares)of identified land and various joint 
inspections were carried out by the officials of Govt. of Puducherry and Govt. of Tamil Nadu. 
5.9.11 After round of deliberations, it is ascertained t hat the Gove rnment of Tamil Nadu is not 
agreeing to bear the cost of land a cquisition for the land fa lling in Tamil Nadu fo r expansion of 
runway. Besides, the Airport  Authority  of  India  has  requested the Government of Puducherry to 
convey the acc eptance of the Revised Master Plan fo r the development of Pudu cherry Airport, in 
which 386 acres of land is e ssentially required, and out of which 272 Acres of land falls with the 
area of Tamil Na du. The Cha irman, Ai rport Authority of I ndia has also requested to c onvey the 
source of funds for the proposed acquisition of land. 
 
5.9.12 A s per the minutes of the Sou thern Zonal Council mee ting dated 30th September, 2020, th e 
Standing Committee has advised the Ministry of Civil Av iation (MoCA) to give f easibility study 
report, i f already prepared, to Gove rnment of Puduc herry, which in turn, will try to identify a 
private partner after floating an Expression of Interest and seeking Viability Gap Funding (VGF), if 
needed. The said report is yet to be furnished by MoCA. Further, as regards to bea ring of cost fo r 
the identified land portions in Tamil Nadu a rea, the Gove rnment of Puduc herry has approached 
Ministry of Civil Aviation (MoCA) and Ministry of Hom e Affairs (MHA) for financial assist ance. 
The Mo CA has info rmed tha t “As  per the Nat ional Civil Av iation Po licy, the land for the 
development of Airport is to be made ava ilable by the State Govt. free of cost and free fro m all 
encumbrances”.The Ministry of Hom e Affairs (MHA) has infor med that “There is no provision to 
provide any s pecific funds for the s pecific project of UT s”. The Gove rnment of Puducherry is 
having limited resources and it is d ifficult to sh oulder the p roposed expenditure of more than Rs. 
225 crore for land acquisition. 
 
5.9.13 The Administration of UT of Puducherry further informed the Committee that the land 
acquisition involve s huge fin ancial imp lications. Unde r these circumstances, the Gove rnment of 
Puducherry may develop the Puducherry Airport under PPP mode. Meanwhile, steps were taken by 
AAI to c onvert Puduc herry Ai rport from Visual Flight Rules (VFR) to In strument Fli ght Rule s 

106 
 
(IFR), for which action has been initiated by AAI. One of the main requirements for this conversion 
is acquisition of a s mall portion of land on both sides of th e present runway. This land is e ssential 
to obtain clearan ce from Director General of Civ il Aviation for obtaining lic ense to operate IFR 
flights with un iform Basic Strip of Runwa y of 140 Mtrs. on e ither side of central line. The land 
portion required for the airport is as below:  
(i) 6.5 a cres of land, which falls under the Government of Tamil Nadu State – 5a cres on 
North & 1.5 acres on South of the Runway. 
 
(ii) 24.75 acres of land, which falls  un der Government  of Puduc herry State - 17.5 acres on 
North & 7.25 acres on South of the Runway 
 
5.9.14 It has been proposed to request the Deputy Collec tor (Revenue) North- cum-L and 
Acquisition Officer to explore the possibilities for acquiring a small portion of the above said l and 
in consu ltation with AAI. For Tamil Nad u po rtion of land, action has to be taken by State 
Government of Tamil Nadu. 
  
5.9.15 The Committee was informed  that total amount of Rs. 1,729.79 crore has been allocated to 
UT of Pudducherry that comprises four sub-heads namely Central Assistance to Union Territory, 
Contribution to Union Territory Disaster Response Fund and token provision under Additional 
Central Assistance to Externally Aided Projects. 
 
5.9.16 The Committee notes that the State Gove rnment of Tamil Nadu is not agreeing to bear 
the cost of land a cquisition for expansion of runway of the Puducherry Airport. Since the 
investment has already been made in completing Phase-I of the project, the Committee,

the project  will  be completed at the earliest.
