RECOMMENDATIONS/OBSERVATIONS - AT A GLANCE  
 
OVERALL ASSESSMENT OF DEMANDS FOR GRANTS 
 
 
Overview
 
 
 The Committee notes that despite COVID-19 Pandemic and lockdowns, the Ministry 
has undertaken some important activities during the year 2020-21. The Committee 
appreciates the achievement of MHA that inter-alia includes establishment of Rashtriya 
Raksha University (RRU) implementation of Emergency Response Support System (ERSS) 
and CCTNS, conducting District Development Council (DDC) elections in Union Territory of 
J&K, Immigration Visa and Foreigners' Registration & Tracking (IVFRT) system, 
resettlement of Bru (Reang), upgradation of forensic capacity or capacity building , 
modernization of Police forces, etc. 
(Para 2.1.14) 
 The Committee takes note of the ongoing vaccination process for COVID-19. and 
observes that, till now, less than 1% of the Indian population has been vaccinated and at  this 
rate it will take many years to vaccinate whole population. The Committee also expresses it s 
concern that a large number of people are missing the second dose of the vaccine. The  
Committee believes that it is very serious issue as the virus is mutating and new variants of 
COVID-19 are being reported in different parts of the world. The Committee there fore, 
recommends that all frontline health workers and the Corona Warriors including CAPFs and 
State/UT Police personnel receive the recommended doses of vaccine.  An attemp t should be 
made to cover maximum people as early as possible. 
(Para 2.1.15) 
  
The Committee also recommends that vaccination in Lakshadweep can be done for 
everyone as the population is very small (70 thousand approx.). As the medical facilities are 
limited, priority needs to be given for vaccination in Lakshadweep. 
(Para 2.1.16) 
 
Allocations, Projections and Variations
 
 
 The Committee notes that there has been allocation of Rs. 1,03,802.52 crore for the 
Police (Demand No.50) at BE 2021-22. The allocation made for the Police (Demand No.50) 
under the Revenue Head is Rs.  94,086.59 crore and under Capital Head is Rs.  9,715.93 crore 
at BE  2021-22. The Committee observes reduction in allocatio n for the Police 
Infrastructure, MoPF, BADP, SRE, Infrastructure for LWE and LPAI in BE 2021-22 o ver 
BE 2020-21. This may hamper the creation of assets and negatively imp act the capabilities 
of the Police forces . The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Minis try should 
reconsider the requirements of the funds for the Po lice and Union Territories and pursue 
with the Ministry of Finance for increased allocati on at the Revised Estimates stage. The 
Committee further recommends that the MHA should also constantly monitor  the 
utilization of funds under different heads on a quarterly basis and ensure op timal utilization 
of funds. 
(Para  2.2.4) 
 

108 
 
 The Committee notes that the total allocation for the Union Territories in BE 2021-22 
is Rs. 55,006.73 that is divided into two parts. i.e. Union Territories (without legislature) is Rs. 
21,562.43 crore and Union Territories (with legislature) are Rs. 33,444.30 crore. Further, for 
the Union Territories (without legislature), the allocation under the Revenue Head is Rs. 
15,657.52 crore i.e. 72.61% of the total allocation to the UTs and Capital Head is Rs. 5,904.91 
crore i.e. 27.38% of the total allocation to the UTs. The Committee is of the considered view 
that the allocation under the Capital Head for the Union Territories (without legislature) 
should be increased for infrastructure in Health, Education, Agricultural, Energy and Allied  
sectors, Transport (connectivity), Development of Tourism, etc., in the UTs. The Committe e, 
therefore, recommends that the Ministry must review the capital requirements of these UTs  
and demand appropriate enhancement at RE stage to carry out developmental work s in the 
UTs. 
(Para 2.2.5) 
 
 The Committee notes that up to 30 th January, 2021 the Ministry of Home Affairs was 
able to spend only 59.81 percent of the total allocation granted under the combined Capi tal 
Head of Demand Nos. 48 and 50 in RE 2020-21. The Committee is disappointed to 
note the persistent under -utilization of funds unde r Capital Head especially 
under Demand No. 50 (Police) for years. The Committee understands that last year i. e. 2020, 
the projects got hampered due to the COVID-19 Pandemic and lockdowns. Therefore, the 
Committee recommends the Ministry should undertake corrective measure s to address the 
issues underlying the poor capital expenditure and improve the utilization of funds. 
(Para 2.2.7) 
 
  
 The Committee notes that all UTs without legislatu re have utilized around 62.25% 
of the total allocation made to them in RE 2020- 21. The Committee is aware that the UT of 
Ladakh was created last year and a separate budget allocation was made for its all- round 
development. But, the Committee is disappointed to note that in its first budgetary allocation, 
the UT of Ladakh has utilized just 27.42% of the allocation made in RE 2020-21. The 
Committee, therefore, recommends that the MHA should closely monitor the fund utilization 
by the UT of Ladakh in the ensuing year and take necessary corrective steps for enhance d 
utilization of funds in the UT. The Committee is of the strong view that the Union Ter ritories 
of Andaman & Nicobar Islands and Chandigarh have optimally utilized the funds allocated 
to them and, therefore, should get their projected allocations from the Ministr y of Finance. 
The Committee further recommends that the Ministry of Home Affairs may review the fu nd 
utilization status of every UT periodically and accordingly request additional funds from the 
Ministry of Finance at the RE stage. 
(Para 2.2.9) 
 
 
Demand No. 48 - Ministry of Home Affairs
 
 
Allocation and Expenditure
 
  
The Committee notes that the expenditure incurred upto 31 st January, 2021 of the 
current financial year 2020-21 under Relief and Rehabilitation for Migrants and 
Repatriates, Infrastructure for Disaster Management, National Cyclone Risk Mitigation 
Project and Other Disaster Management Schemes is quite less than the allocation at RE 
stage. The Committee understands that due to COVID-19 Pandemic, projects were affected. 
The Committee recommends that Ministry should now speed up the projects and ensure full 

109 
 
utilization of the allocated fund under these Heads in the ensuing year. 
(Para 3.2.2) 
 
Official Language
 
 The Committee notes that no survey has been done to find out the perc entage of the 
population speaking Hindi in the country. The Committee, therefore, recommend s that the 
Department of Official Language may take up with the concerned authority to conduct a 
survey to collect data on the number of people reading, writing and speaking Hin di. A survey 
may also be conducted to check the impact of Hindi Pakhwada in promoting Hindi language 
which is organized every year across the country. 
(Para 3.3.5) 
 
 The Committee recommends to create a dictionary of Indian languages incor porating 
the common/synonymous words used in Hindi and other different Indian languages. An 
expert Committee of eminent linguists and lexicographers may be formed to search and select 
common/synonymous words of different languages for the dictionary. 
(Para 3.3.6) 
 The Committee is of the view that the translation done in standard Hindi language is 
very tough to understand and interpret by a common man. Therefore, the Committee 
recommends that the Department of Official Language may review the translation works 
done in Hindi and take necessary steps to ensure that the Hindi translation is done in simp le, 
easy to comprehend and reader-friendly manner.  
(Para 3.3.7) 
Monitoring of disbursal of funds
 
 
 The Committee notes that the Ministry of Home Affairs had instructed all the State 
Governments to mandatorily/necessarily disburse the relief through Direct Benefit Transfer 
(DBT) into the account of the beneficiary. The Committee would like to know as to how many 
States are sending the relief through DBT and observes that matter should be pursued  with 
the States that have not come on board for the purpose. The MHA should assist those States, 
if any, who are facing bottlenecks in shifting or adhering to DBT. The Committe e also 
recommends that the list of beneficiaries (District-wise) who have received the relief may b e 
uploaded on „National Disaster Management Information System (NDMIS)‟  in real-time 
which will ensure timely grant of relief to the victims and also report delay, if any, on the 
part of State Government in disbursing the relief. 
(Para 3.6.3) 
 
Glacial Outbursts
 
 
 The Committee notes that Monitoring of Glacial Lake Outbursts Flows (GLOFs) 
requires a network of meteorological and hydrological stations, early warning system 
comprising of remote Automatic Water Level Recorder (AWLR) or Monitoring Stations an d 
Automatic Weather Stations (AWS) with the ability to communicate and transmit data from  
remote stations to the control room etc., for operation and monitoring in real-time data on air 
temperature, precipitation and river discharge through telemetry (V-SAT). 
(Para 3.8.8) 
 

110 
 
The Committee observes that the Himalayan region of Ladakh, Jammu & Kashmir, 
Himachal Pradesh and Uttarakhand are young, fragile and sensitive mountains. Therefor e, 
there is a need to establish and install state-of-the-art infrastructure along with modern  
equipments of proven efficacy and reach that would play very crucial role in mon itoring the 
movement of glaciers in Himalayan region. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the 
Ministry of Home Affairs should emphatically put before the Ministry of Finance its spec ific 
requirements for the purpose and seek appropriate budgetary allocation at  the RE stage. 
(Para 3.8.9) 
 
 In view of the recent severe glacial outburst at Chamoli in Uttarakhand, the 
Committee, strongly recommends that for ensuring safety of the people of Himachal Pr adesh, 
Uttarakhand and Jammu and Kashmir and Ladakh, a network of meteorological and 
hydrological stations, monitoring stations and weather stations be set up at the earliest. The 
Committee, also recommends that an urgent assessment needs to be made and organizations 
like Indian Institute of Remote Sensing, Dehradun, Uttarakhand, may also be tasked to 
conduct glaciological studies and prepare glacial lake inventory for Himalayan region using 
remote sensing and geographical information system (GIS). Besides, the concerned State s 
should be sensitized by the MHA to place adequate early warning mechanism in every district 
so that vulnerable communities can be alerted on the wake of any disaster in future. 
 
(Para 3.8.10) 
 The Committee observes that excessive cutting of trees and use of dynamites on 
Hills/Mountains of the Himalayan region for roads, tunnels, dams and other 
developmental/infrastructural projects is significantly weakening the surrounding hills and 
may be causing an avalanche, landslides, flash floods and other catastrophes. The Comm ittee 
understands that the Himalayas are fragile mountains and geologically and environmentally 
sensitive. Therefore, the developmental projects taking place in the region should be 
considered from the environmental and ecological point of view. The Committee recommen ds 
that the MHA may coordinate with the concerned agencies including NDMA, Ministry of 
Road, Transport & Highways, etc. to immediately ban the use of dynamites for 
developmental projects in the Himalayan region and make judicious use of technology 
particularly in high altitude Himalayan region to minimize the damage to ecology and 
environment. The Committee further recommends to explore technological solutions 
including use of cutting-edge technology and best practices followed by the othe r countries 
for cutting of hills/mountains for various developmental projects. 
 (Para 3.8.11)

 The Committee notes the steps taken for the resettlement of Brus in Tripura. Th e 
Committee would like to know about the progress made in this regard. 
(Para 3.9.4)

Kashmiri migrants. The Committee further notes that the recruitment has been completed  
for the first tranche of 3,000 posts. But only 304 Transit Accommodation units have been 
constructed and the rest is in progress. The Committee, recommends, that the MHA m ay 
expedite the construction of the remaining units within a time frame and without cost 
overruns. 
(Para 3.9.11) 

111 
 
 The Committee notes the steps taken by the MHA to address day-to-day issues at the 
transit accommodation. The Committee, recommends, that the Ministry may fast-track the 
construction of remaining transit accommodations and complete it within a time frame. T he 
Committee also recommends that the team of MHA may undertake a visit to the transit 
accommodation and furnish a status report to the Committee on the prevailing conditions at 
the transit accommodation.  
(Para 3.9.12) 
 
 The Committee notes that the relief has been enhanced from 2,500 to 3,250. But, the 
Committee is of the view that given the historical circumstances and suffering of the 
Kashmiri migrant, the enhanced amount is still meagre. Therefore, the Committee 
recommends that the MHA may reconsider suitably enhancing the relief amount. T he 
subsequent increased budgetary allocation may be requested to the Ministry of Finance at RE 
stage. 
(Para 3.9.13) 
 
 The Committee observes that when Turtuk became part of India in 1971, the 
families have left their land which was on the other side of the Line of Actual Control (LAC) 
and, therefore, is deprived of livelihood and is living under poverty. Therefore, t he 
Committee recommends that the MHA may consider extending the relief and fina ncial 
assistance to the people living in the Turtuk village under the scheme of „Relief and 
Rehabilitation for Migrants and Repatriates‟. 
(Para 3.9.14)

The Committee also notes that there has been signification reduction under Police 
Infrastructure, MOPF, BADP, SRE Infrastructure for LWE and LPAI in BE 2021-22 as 
compared to BE 2020-21. The Committee believes that the Police modernization, Border  
Infrastructure and development and technology up-gradation is a necessity and a con tinuous 
process and their projects/ programme require adequate allocation of funds. Ther efore, the 
Committee expresses its concern on this reduction and recommends that the M inistry should 
demand additional funds from the Ministry of Finance at RE stage. 
(Para 4.2.3) 
 
Central Armed Police Forces
 
 
 The Committee notes that a large number of vacancies are existing in almost all the 
Central Armed Police Forces as well as Delhi Police. The Committee desires that c adre-wise 
breakup of these vacancies may be intimated to the Committee.  The Committee strongly 
recommends that all vacancies in senior grades should be filled up on urgent basis as this  has 
a direct bearing on the decision making of the forces. 
(Para 4.3.24) 
 The Committee recommends that the remaining vacancies in other cadres may also be 
filled within a time frame so as to increase the strength of CAPFs and further enhance their 
capability.  
(Para 4.3.25) 
The Committee also recommends to undertake recruitment drives at appropriately 
located places on urgent basis so that the candidates do not have to travel long distances to 
participate in various stages of recruitment. This will help in saving funds an d also ensure 
easy availability of candidates. 
(Para 4.3.26) 
 
 The Committee takes note that BSF has been directed to take up with the 
Administration of UT of Ladakh regarding the problem of medical testing and issuing of 
Domicile Certificate in recruitment of BSF. The Committee desires to know abou t the 
decision made in this regard. The Committee notes about the recruitment rally in 22 districts 

113 
 
of Jammu, Kashmir and Ladakh Divisions and recommends that more such rallies sh ould be 
conducted in J&K, Ladakh and LWE affected districts to fill the vacant posts. 
(Para 4.3.27) 
 
 The Committee notes that sensitization-cum-awareness workshops have been 
conducted in few airports out of 64 airports where security cover are provide d by CISF to 
sensitize personnel on the special needs of the autistic passengers and equip them with the 
required knowledge, skills and attitude to handle them in the Airport. The Committee, 
therefore, recommends that sensitization-cum-awareness workshop on the special need of 
autistic passengers may be extended to the remaining airports.  
(Para 4.3.28) 
 
 The Committee notes the initiative taken by CISF/Aviation Security Group (ASG) to 
make use of shoulder carried localized public address system and use of recorded audio/video 
messages in Hindi, English and Tamil languages. The Committee recommends to exte nd the 
facility of shoulder carried localized PA system (Public Address system) in the Airports which 
are frequently faced with issue of congestion in the „peak hours‟ caused on account o f 
bunching of flights, lack of space, etc., with recorded audio/video messages in other Indian 
languages. CISF personnel should also be sensitized about the regional languages so that they 
can communicate with the passengers regarding the requirements of pre-embarkation 
security checks for swifter clearance at security check points. 
(Para 4.3.29) 
 
 The Committee observes that different practices are being followed at dif ferent 
airports. For instance at some airports, passengers are asked to remove belts and in other 
airport they are not. Therefore, the Committee, recommends, that a SOP may be f ormulated 
to create uniform system of checking and frisking at all airports throughout the country. 
(Para 4.3.30) 
 
 The Committee notes with disappointment that women presently constitute only 
3.05% of the total posted strength of CAPFs. The Committee recommends to take special  
measures to increase  the representation of women in CAPFs. The Committee also 
recommends that the recruitment of women may be considered on the basis of operational 
requirement which need not be uniform across all forces It should be don e based on 
requirement and utility particularly in border-Guarding forces.  
(Para 4.3.31) 
 
 The Committee notes that as a stop-gap arrangement, I-Sat phones were provid ed to 
CAPFs personnel to communicate with their families. The Committee further notes that as a 
permanent solution for the problem, DSPTs have been re-activated at 719 sites from ou t of 
1047 sites with the help of Bharat Broadband Network Limited (BBNL).The Committee, 
recommends that the DSPTs at remaining 328 sites may be re-activated at the earliest so that 
the CAPF personnel deployed there can regularly communicate with their families and also 
use internet services. 
(Para 4.3.32) 
 
 The Committee notes that the current housing satisfaction level of the CAPFs is 
45.74%. The Committee further notes the current housing satisfaction level of the Sashastra 
Seema Bal (SSB) personnel is as low as 26.17% followed by the Indo Tibetan Border  Police 

114 
 
(ITBP) personnel which is 39.93%. In view of the fact that still there is a shortfall of 1,45,602 
dwelling units for CAPFs, the Committee, recommends that sufficient budgetary allocations 
should be made to provide adequate accommodation to the CAPFs and ongoing con struction 
of 23,973 houses should be completed within a time frame. The Committee is of the 
considered view that it is the duty of Government to provide proper accommodation  and 
satisfaction to the Force personnel who are deployed at tough terrain with harsh climatic 
conditions. 
(Para 4.3.33) 
 The Committee observes that the Defence Forces Personnel in battalions below 
Officers Rank (PBOR) are entitled for 30 days CL and CAPF‟s (i.e BSF, SSB, CRPF, ITBP & 
AR) personnel in battalions of all ranks are entitled for 15 days CL in a year. The Commi ttee 
notes that CAPFs are getting less leaves as compared to the Army Personnel. They meet 
almost equal operational challenges, hardships and carry equal stress level. Therefore, the 
Committee strongly recommends that the MHA may review the leaves given to the CAPFs to 
ensure that they do not feel discriminated and are abl e to spend adequate time with their 
families. This will also help in reducing stress among the CA PFs personnel.  
(Para 4.3.34 ) 
 
 The Committee notes that just like Army Personnel, the CAPF personnel are also 
posted in the treacherous, mountainous and snowy terrains. Therefore, CAPFs personnel also 
needs to be given the Risk and Hardship allowances as admissible to Army personnel. The 
Committee, therefore, recommends that the MHA should take up the matter urgently on 
priority basis and extend payment of Risk and Hardship Allowance to the CAPFs personnel 
at par with the Army personnel deployed in similar topographies.  
(Para 4.3.35) 
 
 The Committee observes that in a span of one year (2020), 134 persons have 
committed suicide in CAPFs.  In the CRPF number of persons who committed suicide  is the 
highest i.e. 53.  The Committee strongly recommends that a proper study should be 
conducted to analyse the reasons as to why the Force Personnel are taking such drastic  step.  
Moreover, facilities like adequate leave, communication, better treatment of ailing family 
members, quick disbursement of pensions and „Risk and Hardship Allowances”, etc. should 
be provided to the CAPF personnel as incentives which will help to reduce their stress level to 
a great extent. 
(Para 4.3.36)

Central Police Organizations 
 
Narcotics Control Bureau (NCB)
 
 
 The Committee notes the steps taken by the Narcotics Control Bureau (NCB) at 
national as well as international front to contain drug trafficking in the country. The 
Committee expresses its concern about increasing use of crypto-currencies an d "darknet" for 
drug trafficking.  Further, technology is being leveraged by drug traffickers to maintain 
anonymity and making it difficult to track the movement of drugs. The Committee 
recommends that the MHA may advise NCB to establish a Cyber Wing with Intelligence 
Fusion Centre for effective detection and investigation of drug trafficking cases involving  
crypto-currencies and Dark Net at the earliest. The Committee may be apprised ab out the 
progress made in this regard. 
(Para 4.9.6)

Currently, 427 posts are lying vacant which is 33.43% of the sanctioned strength of 1,277 
posts. The Committee further notes that the efforts are being made to bring more pe ople on 
deputation to NIA. The Committee believes that the vacancies in NIA are of serious conce rn

 The Committee notes that no significant improvement has been made in creat ion of

note that the majority of the Marine Immigration Posts lack basic infrastructure facilities like

is aware that Bureau of Immigration requires closed climate controlled environment along 
with leased line connectivity to conduct its operations. But in some places, Immigration 
Authorities are working in temporary structures and putting health of officials deployed 
there at risk as also exposing technical gadgets to inclement weather.

MHA should make adequate budgetary provision in order to support them. The Commi ttee 
also desires to be apprised about the progress made in this regard.

IVFRT (Immigration, Visa and Foreigners‟ Registration and Tracking) 
 
 The Committee appreciates the installation of e-gates at Delhi and Mumbai airport to 
automate immigration clearance for passengers travelling out of the country without any 
human intervention. The Committee is of the view that in the wake of COVID-19, physical 
distancing has become need of the hour. The Committee, therefore, recomme nds to speed up 
the process of installation of electronic gates at Delhi and Mumbai airport and on successf ul 
completion the electronic gates may be installed at other airports that are receiving large foot 
falls of foreign travelers. The Committee notes that to get immigration clearance at the “e -
gates”, passengers will need to scan their passports and boarding passes themselves. But, 
without proper awareness, the facility could lead to congestion at the gates instead. The 
Committee, therefore, recommends that the airport staff should provide guidance to 
passengers regarding the new process and instructions with regards to the use of electronic 
gates may be displayed on screens at important visible locations of the airport to ensure swift 
clearance of passengers at these gates. The additional funds, if required, may be sought at the 
RE stage for this purpose.

bullet proof vehicles, jammers, bomb detection  equipments, communication gadge ts like

funds as requested by the State Police Department without any procedural de lay of 
sanctioning of funds by the State Government to the Police Department.

sensitization of Police personnel. The Committee also recommends that it should be ensured 
that each and every Police personnel completes full training and under-training personnel 
particularly constables should not be posted for field duties for long. Special incentives may 
also be provided to rope in and attract quality trainers for Police personnel.

Border Area Development Programme
 
 The Committee appreciates the initiative taken by the Ministry of Home Affairs in 
holding the „Simant Kshetra Vikasotsav- 2020‟ in Dhordo Village, Kutch (Gujarat) on 12 th 
November, 2020 aimed at sensitizing border population about their role in national security 
and development works. The Committee observes that as the people living in border areas  
are well versed with the terrain and other geographical conditions they can be  of immense 
help in providing civil intelligence inputs relating to the area. The Committee, therefor e, 
recommends that the MHA should conduct „Simant Kshetra Vikasotsav-2020‟ from time to 
time in other border areas also. This will make people living in border areas aware of the 
developmental schemes/programmes of the Governments which are aimed at providing 
sustainable living and overall development of border areas. 
(Para 4.20.9) 
 
 The Committee notes that the revised  BADP guidelines have been framed in the year 
2020 which intends to cover 0-50 km area from the Zero line. The Committee e xpresses its 
concern that currently, some border villages of Ladakh have not been covered   under the 
scheme of BADP. Therefore, the Committee recommeds that the MHA take up the matter to 
include all border areas of Ladakh including hamlets and other barren lands with small 

120 
 
population as per the revised BADP guidelines so that they get the benefits of 
schems/programme under BADP. 
(Para 4.20.10) 
 
The Committee notes that out of total 236 habitable villages, 172 villages have telecom 
infrastructure and only 24 and 78 villages have 3G and 4G internet connectivity, respectively, 
which is very low. The Committee understands the importance of proper communicatio n 
facilities including internet has grown considerably over years and has taken center stage 
during the COVID-19 Pandemic when students have been receiving education through onli ne 
mode due to closure of schools. The Committee also recommends to electrify all vil lages in 
Ladakh particularly, the villages that are located in Zero-Border like Chumar and Demchuk 
in order to stop migration of people from these areas. This will go a long way in fulfilling the 
developmental needs of these remote yet strategically important localities. 
(Para 4.20.11) 
 
DEMAND NO. 51 TO 58 - UNION TERRITORIES
 
 
 
Demand No.51-Andaman and Nicobar Islands
 
 
Cellular Jail  
 The Committee notes the efforts made by the Administration of UT of  A&N Island s 
for documentation of freedom fighters. The Committee recommends that a biograph ical 
sketch may be created of all freedom fighters who were imprisoned in cellular jail during the 
course of freedom struggle so as to acquaint the incoming tourists of the history associated 
with the freedom struggle. 
(Para 5.2.5) 
 
Integration of tribals in the mainstream
 
 
 The Committee is anguished to note that no expenditure was made under „Welfare of 
Tribal  and Other Backward Classes‟  in year 2020 -21 (till 31 st December, 2020) against the 
allocated amount of 0.76 crore in BE 2020-21. The Committee observes that without utilization  
of funds how the UT Administration is going to conserve the vulnerable tribal groups in the UT. 
The Committee recommends that budgetary provisions should be fruitfully utilized f or welfare 
and development of tribals particularly PVTGs for conservation of their tradition, culture, 
dialects, languages,etc. The Committee also recommends that stritct vigilence should be put in 
place to ensure that the tourists do not venture into the tribal areas and harm the tribals in any 
way. 
(Para 5.2.13) 
 
New Greenfield Airport at Port Blair 
 The Committee notes that an alternate Greenfield airport has been proposed to b e 
developed at Port Blair to improve connectivity with the mainland. The Committee is aware 
of the ecological sensitivity and strategic sensitivity of the region and is of the view that 
islands don't need large airports or big planes as it will be damaging to the environment. 
Instead, the small planes with carrying capacity of 50-70 passengers are adequate to cater to 

121 
 
the need of tourists and local people.  The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Home 
Ministry may convene a meeting of all the concerned Ministries/Departments to encourage 
use of small planes in A&N Islands which is sustainable.    
(Para 5.2.15) 
Demand No. 52 -Chandigarh
 
 
 The Committee notes that existing route from Tribune Chowk to Chandigarh 
International Airport is causing unnecessary inconvenience to the residents of Chandigarh as 
they have to cover more distance and spend more time to reach the airport. The  Committee 
recommends that the MHA may take up this matter with Ministries of Civil Aviation and 
Defence on priority basis to connect the old and new terminal through an underp ass below 
the existing runway to make it convenient for the people travelling through the Chandigarh 
Airport. Alternatively, the Committee further recommends that the MHA may coordinate 
with Government of Punjab for making provision of approximately 4.7 kms road through 
village Jagatpura for Chandigarh Airport which will reduce the distance to airport by about 
10 km and will save time of large number of people travelling through airport from 
Chandigarh . 
(Para 5.3.4) 
DemandNo.53-Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu
 
 
The Committee recommends that the MHA along with the UT Administration may 
construct bridges between Vanakbara and Kotda in Diu, Kalai and Jampore and Kolak and 
Nani Daman, both in Daman. 
(Para 5.4.15) 
 The Committee notes that the area of Daman is 72 sq. kms which contains marshy 
land, funnel area, mangroves, rivers and the like.  Other than these areas, the remaining land 
available for development is only 22 sq. kms.  The area available in Diu is still less. Therefore,  
the Committee recommends that that entire Daman be declared under Coastal Regulation 
Zone II (CRZ II) instead of the existing Coastal Regulation Zone III (CRZ III) and Diu as an 
Island. The Committee also recommends that a ring road may be constructed in Daman, for 
which additional funds may be requested at RE stage. 
(Para 5.4.16) 
 The Committee recommends that the MHA may take up with the Ministry of 
Railways for stoppage of the Mumbai New Delhi Rajdhani Express (12951/52)  at Vapi Station 
as it caters to the needs of Vapi, Silvassa, Daman and other nearby industrial town. 
(Para 5.4.17) 
 The Committee notes that Dadra & Nagar Haveli predominantly is tribal area of the 
UT of Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu. Therefore, the Committee recommends 
promotion and incentivisation of the arts and crafts of these tribal communities will help in 
generation of revenue and jobs for them. The Committee also recommends to give reservation 
to the residents of the UT for group C & D posts of various departments.  
(Para 5.4.18) 
 
 
 
 

122 
 
Demand No. 54 –Ladakh 
 
Under-utilization of funds
 
 
 The Committee is disappointed to note that only 27.42% of funds allocated to the UT 
of Ladakh in BE 2020-21 has been spent which is abysmally low. The Committee is aware 
that due to COVID-19 Pandemic restrictions, the progress in various developmental work s 
have been affected. But other UTs (without legislature) in similar situation have utilized more 
than 60% of their allocations. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the MHA may 
take up with the UT Administration to remove bottlenecks and ensure optimal utilization of 
funds in the UT from ensuing year. The Committee, recommends that the special  
development package (SDP) may be made non-lapsable for few years to cater to the 
developmental need of newly created UT.  
(Para 5.5.6) 
Tourism
 
 The Committee notes that Rs. 4 crore was allocated in FY 2020-21 for prese rvation of 
Gompas and Monasteries in the UT.  The committee recommends that the UT may chalk out 
plan in the ensuing year to utilize the allocated fund for development of old Gompas and 
Monasteries. It will help in conservation of cultural heritage, attract tourists and thereby act  as 
a source of revenue for local people. 
(Para 5.5.10) 
Delegation of Power
 
 The Committee notes that setting up of administrative institutions and infrastructure and 
delegation of powers to the engineering cadre have been made in the UT of Ladakh . The 
Committee urges to clearly demarcate role and responsibilities of the institutions for de legation 
and division of work and prevent cascading or overlapping of work in the newly created UT. The 
Committee, recommends that the elected representatives should be involved in each step  of 
developmental plans including  policy/programmme formulation, implementation and 
monitoring. The Committee further recommends to increase sanctioning powers delegat ed to the 
engineering cadre and financial power of the Ladakh Autonomous Hill Development Councils. 
(Para 5.5.13) 
 
 
Demand No. 55 - Lakshadweep
 
 
Transport
 
 
 The Committee is of the view that turboprops aircrafts with 40-70 seating capacity 
require very short take-off and landing strips and are economically viable. As such, they ar e 
ideally suited for the islands and there is no need to land heavy aircrafts and airbuses in the 
Islands.  Therefore, the focus should be on optimal use of the small aircrafts with STOL 
(short takeoff and landing) to promote tourism.  The Committee recommends to revisit the  
proposal for extension of runway for landing Airbus as it is not economically viable and in the 
ecologically sensitive areas it may not be advisable. The Committee also recommends to take 
appropriate steps for attracting private operators for heli-tourism as it will help tourist to 
move swiftly from one island to other and also enjoy the aerial view of beautiful land scape of 
the islands. 
(Para 5.6.9) 

123 
 
Monitoring of Shipping/Harbor Development Works 
 The Committee notes that the Administration of the UT of Lakshadweep does not 
have any control over the implementing agency of the Harbour Development. The Comm ittee 
is of the view that that a separate Division of Andaman and Lakshadweep Harbour Works 
(ALHW) should be established for gaining efficiency in Harbour Development and 
maintenance in Lakshadweep. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the MHA may 
delegate control of the project to the Administration of the UT of Lakshadweep on a priority 
basis. This will ensure the role of UT Administrator in discharge of its duties and justify 
proper utilisation of funds allocated to the UT. 
(Para 5.6.14) 
Internet connectivity
 
 The Committee notes the undersea cable connectivity project for the UT of 
Lakshadweep to provide unlimited broadband connectivity which is proposed to be 
completed by 2022. Due to COVID-19 Pandemic, education has been shifted to online  mode 
which requires good internet connectivity to attend online classes and watch videos. Further, 
internet facility is also very important in the UT for internal security purpose given its 
proximity to Maldives. The Committee, therefore, recommends that till the completion of 
under sea cable project, interim arrangements should be made for providing  internet facility 
in the UT so that the students may continue with their online education and internal security 
measures remain intact. 
(Para 5.6.20) 
Power
 
 The Committee observes that currently, the power generation in the UT is diesel 
based.  The spillage of diesel stored in power houses directly goes to the groundwater an d 
affects the people living around. Therefore, it is important to shift from diesel base power 
generation to renewable power generation. Considering the small size of the UT, it can b e 
fully powered through green energy.  Therefore, the Committee recommend s that the 
Administration of UT of Lakshdweep may speed up the ongoing projects for developmen t of 
renewable energy in the UT also make plans for solar rooftops, solar panels on lagoons, etc. 
(Para 5.6.23) 
Fisheries 
 
 
 The Committee observes that the fishing potential of the UT of Lakshdweep is not  
being harnessed properly. The fishermen are not catching enough fish  due  to lack of 
infrastructure like small crafts, ice plant, cold storage, processing units, etc. to catch, preserve 
and process fishes before they are sold in the market. The Committee is of the view that  
proper storage and processing unit will give fishermen the confidence that if the c atch is not 
sold, at least it can be preserved, and can be sold in the market the next day. T he Committee, 
therefore, recommends that investments should be made for creation of basic in frastructure 
to boost fishing industry in the UT to provide income security to the fishermen. The 
Committee is of the view that inland fisheries too have vast scope for the development of 
fisheries sector in the UT. The Committee, therefore,  recommends to utilize the avail able 
inland resources like ponds, swamps and low lying rain fed areas,  for fresh  water fish 
farming through mariculture, cage culture and sea weed culture The Committee also 
recommends to increase budgetary allocation to develop fishery industry in the UT. 
(Para 5.6.28) 

124 
 
Smart City Project at Kavaratti 
 The Committee notes that Kavaratti island has been declared as Smart City Pr oject. 
The Committee further notes that some part of the project will be funded  directly by 
Kavaratti Smart City Limited (KSCL) and remaining projects will be funded either from 
concerned Central Ministry or UT Administration Department. Due to funding of projec t 
from multiple sources, the progress of work has been very slow. The Committee, ther efore, 
recommends that the MHA may take up the matter with Ministry of Urban Development and 
UT Administration to bring clarity on funding of the project. A decision may also be taken 
that which part of the project will be funded by whom so that the projects that are being 
delayed due to non-availability of funds may take off at the earliest. 
(Para 5.6.30) 
Health Infrastructure
 
 The Committee takes note of the health infrastructure in the hospitals of the UT. The 
Committee further notes that there is no MRI machine in the UT. The Committee, 
recommends that basic minimum diagnostic infrastructure like Ultrasound Scanner, CT 
scanner, MRI scanner, etc., should be arranged at least for a bunch of island s so that doctors 
can examine patients properly and patients may not have to go too far to avail medical 
facilities. The Committee also recommends that adequate budgetary allocations may be made 
for the same. 
(Para 5.6.32) 
 
 The Committee recommends that the MHA may hold periodic meetings on 
development of Lakshadweep as it is a highly eco-sensitive region. The MHA may take up  
matter of structured planning and overall economic development with the UT 
Administration, concerned agencies including the Ministry of Shipping, Member of 
Parliament of the UT and other elected representatives. This will also help in  finding a way 
forward and amicable solution to the developmental problems of the UT. The Committee, 
further recommends that additional funds may be sought from the Ministry for Finance at 
RE stage to carry out the developmental projects of the UT of Lakshadweep. 
(Para 5.6.33) 
 
Demand No. 56 - Transfers to Delhi
 
 
 The Committee expresses its concern about the landfill sites at Bhalsawa and Gazipu r, 
which have been repeatedly expressed over the years.  The Committee observes that these 
sites continue to smoulder from time to time and releases toxic gases. Apart from stubble 
burning, landfill sites are also one of the factors for pollution of the air and the poor air 
quality in the National Capital.  The Committee recommends to increase the number of 
trommels to further reduce the height of dump at the landfill site of Bhalsawa and  Ghazipur 
and allocate adequate funds for the same.  
(Para 5.7.5) 
 
 The Committee also notes that EDMC has signed a MoU with NHAI to use soil like 
material in their road construction in 2016. But no progress has been made so far. T he 
Committee, therefore, recommends that the MHA may take up the matter with NHAI on 
priority for making arrangements to use the waste materials for road construction as EDMC 

125 
 
is densely populated and in absence of filling of this soil like inert mate rial in such a hug e 
quantity, pollution will become more severe in the adjoining areas. 
(Para 5.7.6) 
 
Demand No. 57 - Transfers to Jammu & Kashmir 
 
 The Committee notes that School Education, Health, ICDS, Rural Development, Social 
Welfare and other Departments have been transferred to Panchayat Raj Institu tions at 
various levels along with financial powers.  The Committee is of the view that adequate 
delegation of power should be made for the grassroots institutions of Panchayats.  Therefore, 
the Committee recommends to devolve more funds, functions and functionaries to the 
Panchayats in the UT to bring it at par with Panchayats of the other States/UTs. 
(Para 5.8.6) 
 
Air Connectivity 
 
 
 The Committee notes that Srinagar has an International Airport, but currently, no 
international commercial flights are operating from the airport. The Committee beli eves that 
in absence of direct international flights to Srinagar, the real tourism potential of the UT may 
not be realized to its full potential as international tourists have to take connecting flights to 
reach Srinagar which is expensive and time consuming. Therefore, the Committee 
recommends that the MHA may take up the matter with Ministry of Civil Aviation and take 
appropriate steps to remove bottlenecks and commence international commercial f lights 
operations from Srinagar Airport at the earliest. 
(Para 5.8.9) 
Tourism
 
 
  The Committee notes that in some tourist areas like Gulmarg, Pahalgam and 
Sonmarg, local sightseeing is being done by local taxi operators only.  The Committee 
observes that at times, tourists are being charged exorbitant rates by local taxi operators in 
these places. Therefore, the Committee recommends that the Administration of UT of J&K 
may issue tariffs for hiring taxis to visit tourist places and put a ceiling on maximum rate  that 
can be charged from customer. 
(Para 5.8.12) 
Infrastructure
 
 The Committee is aware of the unregulated blasting of dynamite being done in 
Himalayan region for major projects. The Committee is of the view that it is important to 
ensure that the developmental projects are not disturbing the nature and people li ving in 
these areas. The Committee strongly recommends for forbidding the use of dynamite s in 
Himalayan region. The Committee reiterates its recommendation given at para 3.8.11 of 
chapter III. 
(Para 5.8.16) 
Health and Education
 
 The Committee notes that internet facility was not available in the UT of J&K and h as 
been restored w.e.f. 5 th February, 2021. The Committee observes that the schools have 
remained shut for a long time due to COVID-19 Pandemic. The education was shif ted from 
offline to online mode. But, in absence of internet in the UT, even the students having laptops/ 

126 
 
mobile phones were unable to access online classes.  The Committee is of the view that 
internet plays a crucial role in ensuring that the flow of education remains u ndisturbed and 
the process of learning does not stop even during a Pandemic. The Committee, there fore, 
recommends that the MHA may consult with the Ministries of Education, Electronics & 
Information Technology and other concerned agency is to prepare a blueprint to protect the 
right to education of children during a Pandemic or any other extraordinar y circumstances 
with frequent internet shutdowns. 
(Para 5.8.21) 
 
Demand No. 58 - Transfers to Puducherry 
 
 The Committee notes that against projected demand of Rs. 1,873.02 crore, Rs. 1,729.29 
crore has been allocated to the UT of Puducherry in BE 2021-22. It might create shortage of 
funds for ongoing/proposed projects/schemes. The Committee, therefore, recommen ds that 
the MHA may take up with the Ministry of Finance for enhanced allocation for UT at RE 
stage. 
(Para 5.9.4) 
 
Coastal erosion
 
 
 The Committee notes that the beach of Puducherry town has been com pletely lost due 
to erosion. The Committee also notes that Government of Puducherry has taken several 
measures to address the issue. The Committee, recommends, that adequate budgetary 
allocation may be made for the same and the beach may be restored to within a stipulated 
time frame. The Committee would like to be apprised about the progress made in this regard. 
(Para 5.9.8) 
 
Land issues with Tamil Nadu for Common Airport
 
 
 The Committee notes that the State Gove rnment of Tamil Nadu is not agreeing to bear 
the cost of land a cquisition for expansion of runway of the Puducherry Airport. Since the 
investment has already been made in completing Phase-I of the project, the Committee, 
strongly recommends that the MHA may take up the matter wit h both the Government of 
Tamil Nadu and Administration of UT of Puducherry to sol ve the issue of land acquisition so 
that the valuable resources do not go waste.  Further, fund allocation may also be done so that 
the project will be completed at the earliest.   
(Para 5.9.16)

2. At the outset, the Chairman welcomed the Members of the Committee to the meeting and 
informed them about the agenda of the meeting i.e. Presentation of Home Secretary on Demands 
for Grants (2021-2022) of Ministry of Home Affairs followed by discussion thereon.   
 
(On arrival of witnesses) 
3. The Chairman welcomed Shri Ajay Kumar Bhalla, Home Secretary, Secretary, Official 
Language, and other senior officers of the Ministry of Home Affairs to the meeting of the 
Committee. 
4.       The Home Secretary briefed the Committee about the Demands for Grants (2021-22) of the 
Ministry of Home Affairs under Demand Nos. (48-58),  including 7 CAPFs, Police and Union 
Territories.   He informed that in the current year two additional Grants have been created viz. 
Grant No. 54 for Union Territory of Ladakh and Grant No. 57 for the Union Territory of Jammu & 
Kashmir for allocation of funds to the newly created Union Territories of Ladakh and Jammu and 
Kashmir.   He informed the Committee that due to COVID-19 Pandemic, there was reduction in 
allocation under some Heads in RE 2020-21. He also highlighted the work done by the MHA 

131 
 
during 2020-21  which inter-alia  includes  management of COVID-19 Pandemic and related 
issues; establishment of Rashtriya Raksha University (RRU) and National Forensic Sciences 
University; implementation of Safe City Project; Sanctioning of Indo-China Border Road Phase -II 
Programme; settlements of Bru migrants in Tripura; conduct of District Development Council 
elections in J&K; modernisation of immigration services; promotion of Hindi language; Scheme of 
Assistance to State for Modernisation of Police forces; Ayushman CAPF; etc.     
5.      Some queries were raised by the Chairman and Members of the Committee pertaining to 
operationalisation of NATGRID, provisions for online filing of First Information Report  (FIR), 
implementation status of Aapda Mitra Pilot Scheme, National Seismic Risk Mitigation Programme 
(NSRMP), construction of Intelligence Bureau offices, upgradation/development of infrastructure 
at Marine Immigration Check posts, reasons for reduction in allocation  under „Police 
Infrastructure‟, Assistance to States for Modernization of Police, increase of quota for cadre 
officers upto the rank of ADG   in all the CAPFs, reasons for increase in  number of suicides by the 
CAPF personnel, status of implementation of the Armed Forces Special Powers Act (AFSPA), 
COVID-19 vaccination programme,  police infrastructure, utilization of funds allocated for 
disasters management, units/flats constructed and relief for J&K migrants, disposal of enemy 
properties in India, 
  Freedom Fighters Pension Scheme, fencing, flood lighting and construction of 
roads at international borders,  communication facility to CAPFs personnel in high altitude areas, 
Border Area Development Project (BADP) in Ladakh, Glacier outburst and other calamities in 
Himalayan region,  monitoring mechanism to deal with harassing and killing of Indian fishermen 
by Sri Lankan navy, etc. 
6.  Some of the queries raised were replied by the Home Secretary and other concerned 
officers. On other queries, the Ministry was asked to furnish written replies to the Secretariat. 
 
(The witnesses withdrew and the Committee then adjourned for lunch at 1.15 p.m. and re-
assembled after lunch at 2.00 p.m. to discuss Demand No.50 pertaining to CAPF and Delhi 
Police) 
7.   The Chairman welcomed Home Secretary, Directors General of all the Central Armed Police 
Forces (CAPFs), the Commissioner, Delhi Police and other officials to the meeting.  He requested 
the Home Secretary to make a presentation on Demands for Grants (2021-2022) of Ministry of 
Home Affairs pertaining to Central Armed Police Forces and Delhi Police. 
8.   The Home Secretary briefed the Committee about the Demand for Grants (2021-22) for the 
Police (including Central Armed Police Forces and Delhi Police) under demand No. 50 which 
included the allocations made for seven CAPFs and Delhi Police; 'Ayushm an CAPF' ‟under the 
'Ayushman Bharat Scheme' for cashless treatment; National Intelligence Grid (NATGRID); Central 
Police Organizations (CPOs); modernization of police forces; border infrastructure and 
management; police infrastructure; safety of women; etc.    Presentations on CAPFs and Delhi 
Police, were made by the respective representatives. 
9. Queries were raised by the Chairman and Members of the Committee which inter-alia 
include leave policy of Central Armed Police Force (CAPF) personnel, installation of CCTVs in 
Police Stations, non-utilization of allocation for police reforms, community policing, reactivation of 
digital satellite phone terminals, different SOPs for CISF personnel regarding checking of Airline s 
passengers across the country,  welfare measures taken for the CAPF Personnel and their families; 
vacancies in CAPFs; reservation and representation of women, SCs and STs in CAPFs; hardship 
allowance given to CRPF personnel, recruitment drive by BSF in Ladakh,  low housing satisfaction 
level among the Delhi Police personnel; representation of women in Delhi Police; etc. 
 

132 
 
10.  Some of the queries raised were replied by the Home Secretary and other concerned 
officers. On other queries, the Ministry was asked to furnish written replies to the Secretariat. 
                                                   (Witnesses withdrew) 
11. A Verbatim record of the proceeding of the meeting was kept. 
12. The Committee, then, adjourned at 03:39 p.m.

SECRETARIAT

 respectively.

3. The Chairman welcomed Shri Govind Mohan, Additional Secretary (UT), MHA and other 
senior officers of the Ministry of Home Affairs and the representatives of the Union Territories of 
Chandigarh, Lakshadweep, Delhi and Puducherry to the meeting of the Committee and asked them 
to make presentation on the Demand for Gants 2021-22. 
4.       Thereafter, the representative of MHA made a detailed presentation that inter-alia included 
projected amount and Budget Estimates for the UTs in the year 2021-22, utilization of funds by 
UTs in 2020-21, infrastructure and other developmental projects undertaken/proposed/completed in 
the UTs of Chandigarh, Lakshadweep, Delhi and Puducherry. 
5.  Some queries were raised by the Chairman and Members of the Committee pertaining to the 
expansion of Agatti airport; replacing diesel power generation from renewable energy; steps taken 
for preservation  of the fragile coral ecosystem in the Islands of Lakshadweep; internet c onnectivity 
in the Islands of Lakshadweep; creation of infrastructure for  development of fisheries in 
Lakshadweep; Smart City Project at Kavaratti ; health Infrastructure in Lakshadweep; coastal 
erosion in Puducherry; land issues with Tamil Nadu for common airport in Puducherry;   Central 
Assistance given to Union Territory of Delhi for financing of UT projects/schemes; landfill sites at 
Bhalsawa and Ghazipur in  UT of Delhi; improvement in public transportation and parking  in 
Delhi; monitoring of shipping/harbour development works in Lakshadweep;   up-gradation of 
stadium and complex in the UT of Chandigarh;  construction status of Sardar Beant Singh 
Memorial Project; construction of accommodation for judges in UT of Chandigarh, approach road 
to Chandigarh Airport; and work progress of Zirakpur-Tribune road; etc.  
 
6. The Chairman and Members of the Committee gave some suggestions that inter-alia 
included alternate road to Chandigarh International Airport through village Jagatpura in Punjab to 
reduce the distance to airport; land a cquisition for expansion of runway of the Puducherry Airport; 
use of turboprops aircraft with 40-70 seating capacity requiring very short take-off and landing 

135 
 
strips in the ecologically sensitive islands of Lakshadweep; and delegation of monitoring of 
shipping/harbour development works to Lakshadweep Administration; etc. 
7.  Some of the queries raised were replied to by the representatives of the Ministry of Home 
Affairs and Union Territories of Chandigarh, Lakshadweep, Delhi and Puducherry. On other 
queries, the Ministry was asked to furnish written replies to the Secretariat within 3 days. 
                                                    (Witnesses withdrew) 
8. A Verbatim record of the proceeding of the meeting was kept. 
9. The Committee, then, adjourned at 12:45 p.m.

2. At the outset, the Chairman welcomed the Members of the Committee to the meeting and 
apprised them about the agenda i.e. presentation of Home Secretary on the Demands for Grants 
Nos. 51, 53, 54 and 57 pertaining to the Union Territories of A & N Islands, Dadra & Nagar Haveli 
and Daman & Diu, Ladakh and Jammu & Kashmir, respectively. 
 (On arrival of witnesses) 
 
3.
 The Chairman welcomed Shri Ajay Kumar Bhalla, Home Secretary, other senior officers of 
the Ministry of Home Affairs and the representatives of the Union Territories (UTs) of Andaman & 
Nicobar Islands, Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu, Jammu & Kashmir and Ladakh to the 
meeting of the Committee and asked them to make presentation on the relevant Demands For 
Grants (2021-22). 
4. Thereafter, the representative of MHA made a presentation that inter-alia included 
projected amount and Budget Estimates in the year 2021-22, utilization of funds in the previous 
year i.e. 2020-21, infrastructure and other developmental projects undertaken/proposed/completed 
in the UTs of A&N Islands, Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu, Ladakh and Jammu & 
Kashmir. 
5. After the presentation, certain queries were raised by the Chairman and Members of the 
Committee that inter-alia included issues pertaining to the number of tribes and their population in 
A&N Islands; measures taken to protect population; culture and tradition of particularly vulnerable 
tribe groups in A&N islands; Under-utilization of funds in Ladakh; progress made in construction 
of Zoji-la tunnel; strengthening of administrative institution in Ladakh; BSF recruitment rally in 
Ladakh; division of power between Panchayati Raj Institution; Autonomous Hill Development 
Council and UT Administration of Ladakh; preservation of old Gompas in Ladakh; Non-lapsable 
Central Pool of Resources for UT of Ladakh;  rehabilitation of Kashmiri migrants;  number of 
private hospitals and educational institutions in J&K ; exorbitant rates charged by local taxi 
operators in tourist places in J&K; industrial development in J&K ; proposal for the Assembly 
elections and granting Statehood to J&K ; use of dynamites in Himalayan region; expansion of 
airport in  A&N Islands; environmental and eco sustainability of the infrastructure and 

138 
 
developmental projects in A&N Islands; development of tourism in A&N Islands; and progress 
made in proposed Bilaspur-Leh rail link; etc.   
6. The Chairman and Members of the Committee gave some suggestions that inter-alia 
included the creation of a biographical sketch of all freedom fighters who were imprisoned in 
cellular jail in A& N Islands; integration of tribals in the mainstream in A&N Islands, use of small 
aeroplanes in A&N Islands; construction of bridges in Daman; declaration of entire Daman under 
Coastal Regulation Zone II (CRZ II) and Diu as an Island; promotion of the arts and crafts in J&K 
and Ladakh; allocation of funds for the preservation of Gompas and Monasteries in the UT; 
delegation of more financial powers to the Ladakh Autonomous Hill Development Councils ; 
banning of unregulated blasting by using dynamite in the Himalayan region for infrastructure and 
developmental projects; availability of high-speed internet in J&K and Ladakh; etc. 
7. Some of the queries raised were replied to by the representatives of the Home Ministry and 
Union Territories. On other queries, the Ministry was asked to furnish written replies to the 
Secretariat within 3 days. 
                                                    (Witnesses withdrew) 
8. A Verbatim record of the proceeding of the meeting was kept. 
9. The Committee, then, adjourned at 01:15 p.m.

5.  The Committee took up the draft 231 st Report for consideration and adoption. The 
Chairman and Members suggested to take necessary steps to remove encroachment and expedite
